ANSWER.md

Decision

Decline the customer-initiated return and escalate urgently to the fraud/security team (expected disposition: *escalated*).

The customer must not send the £750.00 to the account details in the 27 Sep text. The texted details (recorded as SYN-SC-06-3 / SYN-ACCT-06-1, "R James") differ from the originating Meridian Bank account (SYN-SC-06-2 / SYN-ACCT-06-2), the Confirmation of Payee check on the texted details returned ANNM – account name does not match, the text came from an unknown mobile number, and no funds-recovery request has been received from Meridian Bank for end-to-end ID MER26092622143198. The case is treated as a suspected mistaken-payment redirect scam in progress.

Nothing below has been executed. All actions are proposed and pending.

Proposed actions (in order)

  1. s1 — Hold / decline (servicing agent). Decline to initiate, assist or recommend any outbound payment of the £750.00 credit (end-to-end ID MER26092622143198) to the details supplied in the 27 Sep text. Scope is limited to the customer-initiated return; no other account activity is restricted by this step.
  1. s2 — Urgent escalation to the Harrow & Vale Bank fraud/security team (requires s1). Pass: customer Aisha Khan (student current account); inbound FPS £750.00 GBP received 2026-09-26T22:14:31+01:00 from "R JAMES", Meridian Bank, SYN-SC-06-2 / SYN-ACCT-06-2, ref "RENT", end-to-end ID MER26092622143198, recovery_request_received = false; text received Sun 27 Sep 08:41 from +44 7700 900 823 signed "Ryan" asking for payment to an "other" account; CoP on texted details SYN-SC-06-3 / SYN-ACCT-06-1 "R James" = ANNM at 2026-09-28T09:52:40Z; the unresolved discrepancy between the case record (SYN-SC-06-3 / SYN-ACCT-06-1) and the OCR transcript (SYN-SC-06-2 / SYN-ACCT-06-2), with the original screenshot attachments/sms_screenshot.png attached for their review; customer has not spent the funds and has not made any payment. Ask the team to review the inbound credit, decide whether to ring-fence the £750 pending any Meridian recovery request, and handle any liaison with Meridian Bank.
  1. s3 — Reply to the customer in the in-app chat (requires s1). The plan's reply tells the customer that the matter has been referred to the fraud team, so the full draft is held as a future message to be released only after s1 and s2 have been acknowledged as executed. Its required content is summarised below.

Customer message — content to be delivered (pending; not sent)

The reply to the customer, once s1 and s2 are acknowledged, is to cover:

  • Yes, this is a problem, and she did the right thing by asking first. She should not send the £750 herself and should not reply to or act on the text.
  • The account in the text is different from the one the money actually came from, and the bank's check on the texted details shows the name "R James" does not match the account given.
  • The bank has not received any request from the sender's bank to recover the payment, so it cannot confirm that the person texting is the real sender.
  • If this really was a mistake, the proper route is for the sender to contact their own bank (Meridian Bank), which can send a formal recovery request; the bank then deals with it through that process and she does not need to make a payment herself.
  • She should leave the £750 untouched in her account in the meantime and keep the text message.
  • The matter is being referred to the fraud team for review; that does not confirm it is a scam and does not mean the money has been recalled yet; no timescale can be given.
  • Any genuine contact from the bank will come through the app or its published numbers; the bank will never ask for her PIN, password or one-time codes.

The verbatim draft is provided separately as a future message (NOT SENT) conditional on s1 and s2.

Where the plan stops

  • No outbound payment is to be made or facilitated to any account supplied by the texter.
  • The plan does not decide whether the £750 is ring-fenced, whether Meridian Bank will raise a recovery request, or whether the texter is the genuine payer; those depend on the fraud/security team's review and on Meridian Bank.
  • The account-detail discrepancy between the case record and the OCR transcript is handed to the fraud/security team with the original image; it is not resolved here.

Future message draft — NOT SENT

Use only after confirmed execution of: s1, s2.

> Yes, that is a problem, and you've done exactly the right thing by asking first. Please don't send the £750 yourself, and don't reply to the text. The account in the text is different from the one the money actually came from, and when we checked the details in the text, the name "R James" does not match the account they gave you. We also haven't received any request from the sender's bank to recover the payment, so we can't confirm the person texting is the real sender. If this really was a mistake, the right way to fix it is for the sender to contact their own bank (Meridian Bank), which can send us a formal recovery request — we then return it through that process, and you don't need to make a payment yourself. In the meantime please leave the £750 untouched in your account and keep the text message. I've referred this to our fraud team to review; that doesn't mean it's definitely a scam, and it doesn't mean the money has been recalled yet — I can't give you a timescale for that. Any genuine contact from us will come through the app or our published numbers, and we will never ask for your PIN, password or one-time codes.

REASONING.md

REASONING — Case 006: Unexpected incoming £750 credit and request to "return" it to a different account

Full input is saved in context.md. This memo sets out the public grounds for the decision of record; nothing described here has been executed.

1. Situation and history

Authenticated records (bank-side):

  • attachments/inbound_payment_message.json: a Faster Payment of £750.00 GBP was credited to the customer's account on 2026-09-26 at 22:14:31+01:00 from payer "R JAMES", Meridian Bank, sort code SYN-SC-06-2 / account SYN-ACCT-06-2, reference "RENT", end-to-end ID MER26092622143198. The payee is "MISS AISHA KHAN". The field recovery_request_received is false.
  • event-001 (internal account view, 2026-09-28): confirms the credit, states that no inbound funds-recovery request has been received from Meridian, records the text the customer forwarded (quoting the account details as SYN-SC-06-3 / SYN-ACCT-06-1, "R James"), and records a Confirmation of Payee check on those details as No match — the account name held is not "R James". The customer is a 20-year-old student current-account holder (opened 2024) with typical low-value activity.
  • attachments/cop_check_text_details.json: the CoP request was for SYN-SC-06-3 / SYN-ACCT-06-1, name "R James", personal account; response matched: false, reason code ANNM "Account name does not match", checked at 2026-09-28T09:52:40Z.

Customer's report (in-app chat, 2026-09-28):

  • event-002: the customer says someone paid £750 into her account "by accident on Saturday", that they texted asking for it back, and asks whether she can "just do that now". She says she has not touched the money and feels bad because the texter said it was their rent.
  • event-003: the agent acknowledges and says they will look at the payment and the details given.
  • event-004: the customer adds that "they gave me a different account to send it to than the one it came from" and asks whether that is a problem; she wants the money gone because "it's not my money".

The text message (third-party content, not authenticated):

  • attachments/sms_screenshot.png — original image; could not be read directly (unsupported format, no OCR performed on the image by this process).
  • attachments/sms_screenshot.png.ocr.txt — a machine OCR transcript showing a message from +44 7700 900 823, dated Sun 27 Sep 08:41, signed "Ryan", saying the £750 was sent by mistake and was "meant for my landlord", and asking for it to be sent to "my other account, it's easier".

The customer's identity authority is recorded in the plan as established (in-app personal banking chat; event-002, request.md).

2. Evidence and gaps

What is established:

  • The £750 credit, its originating account and the absence of any Meridian recovery request are confirmed by the bank's own records (event-001, attachments/inbound_payment_message.json).
  • A CoP check on the details recorded in the case file for the text returned ANNM at 2026-09-28T09:52:40Z (attachments/cop_check_text_details.json, event-001).
  • The text came from a mobile number the customer does not know, asked for payment to an "other" account, and included an emotional/urgency framing ("meant for my landlord", "I'm in a bind") (event-001, OCR transcript, event-004).
  • The customer has not spent the funds and has not yet made any payment (event-002, event-004).

Contradiction — the account details in the text:

The case record (event-001) quotes the text as giving SYN-SC-06-3 / SYN-ACCT-06-1, and the customer herself says the account is *different* from the one the money came from (event-004). The CoP check was run on those details. However, the machine OCR transcript (attachments/sms_screenshot.png.ocr.txt) reads SYN-SC-06-2 / SYN-ACCT-06-2 — which would be the originating Meridian account. The two versions of the text also differ slightly in wording (the OCR version omits "Really appreciate it, I'm in a bind."). The original image was not read by this process, so the discrepancy cannot be resolved here. The plan records it as an open item and routes the original screenshot to the fraud/security team rather than guessing. Note that even on the OCR reading, the text still asks for payment to "my other account", which is inconsistent with the OCR digits matching the originating account — a further reason to treat the transcript as unreliable and have the image reviewed.

What cannot be established:

  • Whether the sender of the text is the genuine payer "R JAMES". Nothing in the supplied evidence links the mobile number to the Meridian account holder.
  • Whether Meridian Bank will raise a formal funds-recovery request for end-to-end ID MER26092622143198 (none received at decision time).
  • The fraud/security team's assessment, including whether they will ring-fence the £750 or contact Meridian.
  • Whether the £750 was in fact a mistaken payment at all; the "mistake" is asserted only by the texter.

No external policy documents or links were supplied; this memo cites only the plan's rationale and the case materials.

3. Why this plan

The plan's governing judgment is that the risky action in this case is the customer sending the money herself, and that the combination of signals present is concrete warning evidence of a mistaken-payment redirect scam. Each step preserves its own rationale:

s1 — Decline the customer-initiated return (hold_or_decline). *Explanation:* a request to return a "mistaken" credit to an account other than the one it came from, backed by a CoP no-match and with no recovery request from the sending bank, is concrete warning evidence. *Evidence:* event-001, attachments/cop_check_text_details.json, attachments/inbound_payment_message.json, event-004. *Criterion:* treat a pressured redirect request with CoP mismatch as warning evidence; stop the risky proposed action and specify exactly what is on hold. *Source:* this is the fraud-takeover skill's handling rule, not a quoted bank policy clause or a statutory obligation. The step is deliberately narrow: only the customer-initiated return to the texted details is declined; no other account activity is restricted.

s2 — Urgent escalation to the fraud/security team (escalate_urgent, requires s1). *Explanation:* the pattern — unexpected credit, text from an unknown number, redirect to a different account, CoP mismatch, no sending-bank recovery request — warrants an urgent fraud handoff with all identifiers so the team can protect the customer and resolve the account-detail discrepancy from the original image. *Evidence:* event-001, attachments/inbound_payment_message.json, attachments/cop_check_text_details.json, attachments/sms_screenshot.png.ocr.txt, attachments/sms_screenshot.png. *Criterion:* a suspected scam in progress requires hold of the risky action plus urgent fraud/security escalation; name an unresolved record conflict rather than guessing. *Source:* general safety handling rule. The instruction specifies the full data set to pass (customer, payment identifiers, text details, CoP result and timestamp, the OCR-vs-record discrepancy with the original image, and the fact that the funds are untouched) and asks the team to decide on ring-fencing and any Meridian liaison. Decisions about ring-fencing and contacting Meridian are left to that team; the plan does not make them.

s3 — Reply to the customer (reply_to_customer, requires s1). *Explanation:* answers the customer's direct question in event-004 with the recorded facts (different account, CoP no match, no recovery request), gives the supported route for a genuine return (the payer contacts Meridian Bank, which can raise a formal recovery request), and states what the referral does not confirm. *Evidence:* event-004, event-002, attachments/cop_check_text_details.json, attachments/inbound_payment_message.json. *Criterion:* reply with the recorded status and what it does not confirm; never facilitate a transfer to the suspected party; do not promise recall or reimbursement outcomes. *Source:* payments skill handling rule.

Ordering. s1 comes first because it fixes the protective position before any communication or handoff. s2 and s3 both depend on s1. The plan's customer message tells the customer that the fraud referral has been made, so in practice it should be released only once s2 is also acknowledged; for that reason the verbatim draft is returned as a future message conditional on s1 and s2 rather than as a present-state reply.

Alternatives considered within the plan's scope. The customer's own proposal — pay the £750 to the texted details — is the action being declined; the plan explains why (event-004, CoP ANNM, no recovery request, unknown number). Returning the funds to the *originating* account on the customer's own initiative is not part of the plan and is not proposed here; the plan's position is that a genuine return comes via Meridian Bank's recovery process, not a customer-initiated payment. No new strategy is added.

4. Execution boundaries

  • s1 has no dependencies and is the first action. It restricts only the customer-initiated return to the texted details.
  • s2 requires s1. It is a handoff; its outcome (ring-fencing, Meridian liaison, resolution of the account-detail discrepancy) is for the fraud/security team and is not decided here.
  • s3 requires s1. Because the draft refers to the fraud referral as made, it is held as a future message with s1 and s2 as prerequisites. If s2 cannot be executed, the message would need a fresh decision rather than an improvised rewrite.
  • The plan stops after s3. It does not pre-decide what happens if Meridian raises a recovery request, if the fraud team ring-fences the funds, or if the original screenshot shows different account details. Any of those would be new information requiring a fresh decision. escalate_if is empty; no additional conditional branches exist.
  • If any step is blocked, execution should halt and the case should be returned for a new decision; no alternative strategy is to be substituted by this document.

5. Expected result and limits

What the proposed steps could achieve: prevent the customer from making an irreversible outbound payment to unverified details; get the full fact pattern — including the unresolved discrepancy and the original screenshot — in front of the fraud/security team urgently; and give the customer a clear, factual answer with the correct route for a genuine return and the reassurance that she does not need to pay anyone herself.

What remains unconfirmed: whether this is a scam (the plan does not assert it as fact), whether the texter is the real payer, whether Meridian will raise a recovery request or on what timescale, whether the funds will be ring-fenced, and the true account details in the text. The customer should not be told that the money has been recalled, that she is protected from loss, or that the referral has produced any outcome.

Constraints preserved in the customer message. The draft states every required point: do not send the £750 yourself or act on the text; the texted account differs from the originating account and CoP shows "R James" does not match; we cannot confirm the texter is the real sender; the proper route is for the payer to contact Meridian Bank for a formal recovery request; leave the £750 untouched and keep the text; the matter is referred to the fraud team but this does not confirm fraud or a recall; genuine contact comes via the app or published numbers, and the bank will never ask for PIN, password or one-time codes. It does not facilitate a transfer, does not call the texter a scammer or say the payer's account is compromised, does not promise recall, timescale or protection, does not request any credentials, does not disclose the payer's account details beyond what the customer already holds, and does not describe the referral as having produced an outcome.

ANSWER.md

Decision

Disposition: needs_info. The £1,260.00 deposit return is booked and available, but only one of the three requested payments can proceed to secure confirmation now. The other two are held pending the customer's answers.

  • Salvatore Russell — £247.50: Confirmation of Payee matched and the amount reconciles with the agency statement and the customer's own CSV. Proposed to go to the customer for secure in-app confirmation. Not released until she confirms.
  • Jack Elson — amount unresolved: Customer requested £375.00; the agency statement and her own CSV note ("450 − 75 − 37.50") both give £337.50. At £375.00 the four shares total £1,297.50, £37.50 more than the £1,260.00 received. Held until she confirms the amount.
  • Emerald Creagh — £337.50, CoP no match: The receiving bank holds the name as "E CREAGH-DOYLE" (reason ANNM). Held until the customer verifies the details directly with Emerald and explicitly decides whether to proceed.

No payment has been set up or sent. No same-day arrival is promised. The bank takes no view on whether the deductions or the split are fair; it acts only on the customer's instruction from her own account.

Proposed actions (in order, none executed)

  1. s1 — Request secure in-app confirmation (Salvatore Russell). Submit to the customer's secure in-app confirmation: new payee Salvatore Russell, sort code SYN-SC-07-2, account SYN-ACCT-07-1 (CoP match), Faster Payment £247.50 from her personal current account, reference "Deposit 7 Isfield Rd". Payment is not released until she confirms in the app.
  2. s2 — Ask the customer to confirm Jack Elson's amount. £337.50 (agency statement and her own note) or £375.00 (her request); explain the £37.50 over-allocation. Do not alter the amount unilaterally.
  3. s3 — Ask the customer to verify Emerald Creagh's details. Explain the CoP no match and the name held ("E CREAGH-DOYLE"); ask her to check directly with Emerald via a contact method she already uses, then tell us whether she wants to proceed with £337.50 to those details; warn that a payment to wrong details may not be recoverable.
  4. s4 — Reply to the customer in the in-app chat with the message below.

Proposed message to the customer (in-app chat, pending)

> Thanks — the £1,260.00 from Oakfield PM reached your account on 22 September and is available. I've checked your split against the agency statement. Two things before I can send everything: (1) Jack's amount — your request says £375.00 but the agency statement and your own note give £337.50; at £375.00 the four shares come to £1,297.50, £37.50 more than was returned. Please confirm which you want. (2) Emerald — our name check couldn't match "Emerald Creagh" to the account details; that bank holds the name as "E CREAGH-DOYLE". Please check directly with Emerald that these are her details, then let me know if you want to go ahead; if details are wrong the money may not be recoverable. Salvatore's £247.50 matched correctly and I'm sending it to you now to confirm securely in the app — it isn't sent until you confirm it. Once you confirm Jack's amount and Emerald's details, I'll set those up the same way. I can't promise they will arrive today, but I'll move as quickly as possible. I'm not able to comment on the deductions themselves — these payments go on your instruction from your account.

What remains open

  • Customer's confirmation of Jack Elson's amount (£337.50 or £375.00).
  • Customer's confirmation, obtained directly from Emerald Creagh, that SYN-SC-07-1 / SYN-ACCT-07-2 is her account held as "E Creagh-Doyle", and her explicit decision whether to proceed despite the CoP no match.
  • Customer's secure in-app confirmation of the Salvatore Russell payee and £247.50 payment.

REASONING.md

Decision memo — Case 007, sharing a returned deposit (Harrow & Vale Bank plc)

Full input is in the saved context.md. All actions below are proposed; nothing has been executed.

1. Situation and history

Authenticated records (bank side)

  • event-001 (internal account view, 22 Sep 2026): Customer Miya Yagisawa, personal current account, balance before today £412.30. Credit of +£1,260.00 from "OAKFIELD PM CLIENT A/C", ref "DEP RETURN 7 ISFIELD RD", booked 22 Sep 10:05. CoP results for three new payees: Salvatore Russell — match; Jack Elson — match; Emerald Creagh — no match, account name held "E CREAGH-DOYLE".
  • attachments/cop_results.json: confirms the above with the payee details — Russell SYN-SC-07-2 / SYN-ACCT-07-1 matched; Elson SYN-SC-07-3 / SYN-ACCT-07-3 matched; Creagh SYN-SC-07-1 / SYN-ACCT-07-2 not matched, reason code ANNM, name at bank "E CREAGH-DOYLE".

Customer's report (in-app chat, authenticated session)

  • event-002: Customer asks the bank to split the returned deposit and send flatmates' shares: Salvatore £247.50, Jack £375.00, Emerald £337.50, she keeps £337.50. States deposit was £1,800 (£450 each), landlord kept £540. Asks for same-day sending ("Jack's being a pain about it"). Attaches deposit_split.csv.
  • event-003: Servicing agent replies that they will check the amounts against what came in. No payment set up.
  • event-004: Customer says the deductions were agreed in writing by everyone and it "just needs sending".

Supplied documents

  • attachments/agency_deposit_statement.pdf (Oakfield Property Management, ref DEP/7ISF/2026, 22 Sep 2026): deposit £1,800.00; deductions £300.00 garden (shared), £90.00 plumbing (S. Russell only), £150.00 cleaning (shared) = £540.00; £1,260.00 returned to lead tenant 22/09/2026. Per-tenant shares: Yagisawa £337.50, Russell £247.50, Elson £337.50, Creagh £337.50 (sum £1,260.00).
  • attachments/deposit_split.csv: the customer's own split — Russell 247.50 (note "450 - 75 - 37.50 - 90"), Elson 375.00 (note "450 - 75 - 37.50"), Creagh 337.50 (note "450 - 75 - 37.50"), Miya keep 337.50.

2. Evidence and gaps

Established

  • The credit is real, booked and available: event-001 shows £1,260.00 credited at 10:05 on 22 Sep, consistent with the agency statement's "returned to lead tenant" line.
  • The customer is acting in an authenticated in-app personal banking session on her own account (event-002, request.md). Payments from her own account on her own instruction do not require any mandate from the flatmates (plan fact; identity_authority: established).
  • Salvatore Russell: CoP match and £247.50 agrees with both the agency statement and the customer's CSV. No discrepancy.
  • Jack Elson: CoP match, but the amount conflicts. The customer's request (event-002) and CSV amount column say £375.00; the agency statement says £337.50; and the CSV note for Jack ("450 - 75 - 37.50") arithmetically equals £337.50. With £375.00 the four shares total £1,297.50, £37.50 more than the £1,260.00 received. The bank cannot tell whether £375.00 is a typo or a deliberate private arrangement between flatmates.
  • Emerald Creagh: amount £337.50 reconciles, but CoP returns no match (ANNM), with the account name held as "E CREAGH-DOYLE" (cop_results.json, event-001). A hyphenated surname is a plausible benign explanation, but the record does not establish it, and the customer has not been told of the mismatch yet.

Cannot be established from the record

  • Which figure the customer actually intends for Jack.
  • Whether SYN-SC-07-1 / SYN-ACCT-07-2 is genuinely Emerald Creagh's account.
  • Whether the customer, once informed, wishes to proceed with the Creagh payment.
  • The customer's statement that "everyone agreed the deductions in writing" (event-004) is corroborated by the agency statement's note of agreement on 18 Sep 2026, but the bank does not adjudicate the deductions in any event.

No safety flags are raised in the plan; the case is a standard payment instruction with one reconciliation conflict and one failed payee check.

3. Why this plan

The plan preserves the customer's instruction and only holds what cannot be safely executed.

s1 — request_confirmation (Salvatore Russell £247.50). Rationale: the payee is CoP matched and the amount reconciles exactly with the agency statement and the customer's CSV (event-002, cop_results.json, agency_deposit_statement.pdf, event-001). Criterion: identify the exact transaction by reference, amount and parties; a clean, reconciled instruction from an authenticated customer on her own account may proceed to secure confirmation. Source: skill:payments handling rule (not a supplied bank policy or legal obligation). This step does not wait on the other two because nothing about it is unresolved; the secure in-app confirmation remains the customer's own release step, so nothing is sent until she confirms.

s2 — ask_customer (Jack Elson amount). Rationale: the customer's figure for Jack is inconsistent with her own CSV note and with the agency document, and over-allocates the deposit by £37.50 (event-002, deposit_split.csv, agency_deposit_statement.pdf, event-001). Criterion: name an unresolved conflict and request the specific missing fact rather than guessing; do not change an instructed amount unilaterally. Source: general:evidence. The bank must not silently reduce to £337.50 (it may be a deliberate choice; she has funds to cover £375.00) nor silently send £375.00 (it contradicts the documents and her own working). Asking is the only option that neither alters her instruction nor assumes it.

s3 — ask_customer (Emerald Creagh CoP no match). Rationale: an ANNM result means the name does not match the account; the mismatch may be a hyphenated surname but the bank cannot assume that (cop_results.json, event-001, event-002). Criterion: hold the risky action and obtain the customer's informed confirmation when payee verification fails; never assume a name mismatch is benign. Source: general:safety. The instruction asks the customer to verify through a contact method she already uses with Emerald, not via details she was sent, and warns that a wrong-details payment may not be recoverable. This is a safety rule in the plan, not a cited external regulation.

s4 — reply_to_customer. Rationale: give the booked credit status, the exact reconciliation discrepancy, the CoP warning and what remains pending, without claiming any payment has been sent or promising same-day arrival (event-001, event-002, event-004, agency statement, cop_results.json, deposit_split.csv). Criterion: state only the stage the record proves (credit booked; payments not sent) and do not promise arrival times absent an applicable commitment. Source: skill:payments. The message also makes clear the bank does not comment on the deductions themselves.

Alternatives considered and why not adopted. Sending all three today as requested would send Jack an amount contradicted by the documents and send Emerald's payment against a failed payee check — both outside the plan's safety constraints. Holding all three until everything is resolved would delay a payment that is clean and reconciled for no evidential reason. The plan's partial release is the proportionate middle course.

Order. s1–s3 have no execution dependencies on each other (requires: []). In practice s4's chat reply tells the customer that Salvatore's confirmation is being sent to her in-app, so s4 should go out alongside or after s1 is initiated, and it carries the s2 and s3 questions in one message.

4. Execution boundaries

  • s1 stops at the secure in-app confirmation prompt. The £247.50 is not released unless and until the customer confirms in the app.
  • s2 / s3 stop at the questions. No Jack Elson payment is set up at either figure until she confirms the amount; no Emerald Creagh payment is set up until she reports having checked with Emerald and explicitly decides to proceed.
  • s4 is the single customer reply; it must not state that anything has been sent, scheduled or will arrive today.
  • The plan has no escalate_if conditions and no conditional branches. When the customer replies, her answers constitute new information and the next payments need a fresh decision on the record; this memo does not pre-decide them.
  • Nothing in this plan requests or should request PINs, passwords, full card numbers or one-time codes.

5. Expected result and limits

What the proposed steps could achieve: the customer is told the credit is available; the Salvatore payment is put in her hands for secure confirmation and can proceed on her confirmation; the two blockers (Jack's amount, Emerald's payee check) are put to her with the precise figures and the specific action she needs to take.

What remains unconfirmed: whether she confirms the Salvatore payment; which figure she chooses for Jack; whether Emerald's details are verified and whether she chooses to proceed. Arrival timing is not promised for any payment.

Constraints preserved (plan must_state / must_not): the message states the £1,260.00 credit on 22 Sep as available; the agency shares and the £37.50 over-allocation with Jack's note working out to £337.50; the CoP no match with the "E CREAGH-DOYLE" name and the non-recoverability warning; that Salvatore's payment goes to secure confirmation and is not sent until she confirms; and that Jack's and Emerald's payments follow once the two points are confirmed, with no same-day promise. It does not say any payment has been sent, does not send Emerald's payment on the current CoP result, does not send Jack's at £375.00 without confirmation, does not opine on the fairness of the deductions, and does not change any instructed amount.

ANSWER.md

Decision

Decline the Confirmation of Payee (CoP) override and hold the standing order payee change; refer urgently to the fraud/security team; ask the customer to verify with her landlord through a route she already trusted before today's email.

Expected disposition: escalated. Nothing below has been executed yet; every action is proposed.

Proposed actions (in order)

  1. s1 — Hold / decline (Harrow & Vale Bank plc payments servicing). Decline the requested CoP override and place the payee change on hold (from Meridian Bank SYN-SC-08-1 / SYN-ACCT-08-2 to SYN-SC-08-2 / SYN-ACCT-08-1) for Nicole Hart's personal current account. Reason: CoP ANNM — name held at bank is "DLH PROPERTY SERVICES LTD" (business) versus "Miss D L Hanlon" supplied; details arrived from an unfamiliar sender with a different Reply-To and same-day urgency. Leave the existing £1,150 standing order on the 25th exactly as recorded.
  2. s2 — Urgent referral to the fraud/security team. Suspected payment-redirection (rent mandate) scam attempt. Pass: the CoP result (ANNM, DLH PROPERTY SERVICES LTD, business, checked 2026-09-23T10:12:02Z); the forwarded email with headers (From dl.hanlon.property@sender.example, Reply-To dlh.accounts.desk@reply.example, dated 23 Sep 2026 07:48); the customer's note of prior landlord emails from debbie.hanlon@btinternet.example; the standing order on file (£1,150, 25th, CoP matched at creation, last paid 2026-08-25, zero failures); that no payment has gone to the new details and the payee change is held; the customer's request to override. Ask them to assess and advise on any further protection for the 25th collection.
  3. s3 — Ask the customer. Ask Nicole Hart to contact her landlord using a phone number or contact method she already had before today's email (not by replying to the new email, and not via its Reply-To or any number in it) and confirm one thing: whether Debbie Hanlon now receives rent through DLH PROPERTY SERVICES LTD at SYN-SC-08-2 / SYN-ACCT-08-1. Ask her to report back in this chat or via the bank's usual contact route.
  4. s4 — Reply to the customer in the in-app chat (depends on s1 and s2 being actioned first). Draft below.

Proposed message to the customer (in-app chat)

> Thanks for bearing with me. Confirmation of Payee shows the account you've been given is held in the name DLH PROPERTY SERVICES LTD, a business account — not Miss D L Hanlon — so I'm not able to override that mismatch and I'm not going to make the change for now; the request is on hold. I'm also concerned because the email came from a different address to Debbie's previous emails, replies go to yet another address, and it pushes you to pay today — this is a common pattern in rent-redirection scams. It may still be genuine, but we can't tell from the email itself. Nothing has been sent to the new details and your existing standing order is unchanged and still set for the 25th. Please check with Debbie using a phone number or contact you already had before today — not by replying to this email, and not using any details in it — and ask her to confirm whether she's now paid through DLH Property Services Ltd with those exact details. I'm asking our fraud team to review this too; the next steps depend on their review and what Debbie tells you. If she confirms through a trusted route, let us know and we'll pick the change request back up with that confirmation. We'll never ask you for PINs, passwords or one-time codes.

What is not being done

  • No CoP override and no payee change on the customer's assertion alone.
  • No statement that the email is fraudulent, that the landlord is or is not DLH PROPERTY SERVICES LTD, or that the old account is closed.
  • No prediction about whether the 25th payment will succeed or bounce; no promise of reimbursement or a review timescale.
  • No request for PINs, passwords, full card numbers or one-time codes.

REASONING.md

Decision memo — case 008, "New rent payment details"

Full input is saved in context.md. This memo records the public grounds for the plan of record; nothing has been executed.

1. Situation and history

Authenticated records (bank side)

  • event-001 (internal account view, 2026-09-23) and attachments/standing_order_on_file.json: Nicole Hart, personal current account, has an active £1,150 standing order on the 25th to "Miss D L Hanlon", Meridian Bank SYN-SC-08-1 / SYN-ACCT-08-2, reference "RENT 26 BROAD LANE", created 2025-03-10, CoP matched at creation, last paid 2026-08-25, zero failures.
  • attachments/cop_result_new_details.json: CoP on the new details (SYN-SC-08-2 / SYN-ACCT-08-1, name supplied "Miss D L Hanlon", type personal) returned matched: false, reason code ANNM ("Account name does not match"), name at bank DLH PROPERTY SERVICES LTD, account type business, checked 2026-09-23T10:12:02Z.
  • Identity authority is recorded as established (authenticated in-app chat).

Customer's report and supplied material

  • event-002 (in-app chat, contact start 11:10 UK): the customer asks for the standing order to be redirected to SYN-SC-08-2 / SYN-ACCT-08-1, "Miss D L Hanlon", "today" because the payment runs on the 25th, pasting an email received that morning.
  • attachments/landlord_email_as_forwarded.eml: From "Debbie Hanlon" <dl.hanlon.property@sender.example>, Reply-To dlh.accounts.desk@reply.example, dated Wed 23 Sep 2026 07:48:19 +0100, webmail. Body says she has switched banks, the old account "closes today" and anything sent there "will bounce".
  • attachments/customer_previous_landlord_emails.txt (customer's own note): three earlier landlord emails (2025-03-04, 2025-12-18, 2026-06-02) all from debbie.hanlon@btinternet.example — a different address from today's sender and from the Reply-To.
  • event-003: the agent tells the customer the CoP result must be discussed before any change.
  • event-004: the customer says the landlord "did mention going limited at some point", believes it is her, and asks for the mismatch to be overridden because "she's been my landlord for years".

No payment has been recorded as sent to the new details and the payee has not been changed (event-003, attachments/standing_order_on_file.json).

2. Evidence and gaps

What is established

  • The CoP mismatch is a bank-obtained record, not a customer claim: the receiving account is a business account named DLH PROPERTY SERVICES LTD, while the customer was told to pay "Miss D L Hanlon" as a personal payee (cop_result_new_details.json).
  • The existing standing order was CoP-matched at creation and has paid without failure for 18 months (standing_order_on_file.json).
  • The email's sender and Reply-To both differ from the address on every prior landlord email in the customer's note (landlord_email_as_forwarded.eml vs customer_previous_landlord_emails.txt), and the message imposes same-day pressure.

What is asserted but not verified

  • That the landlord has "gone limited" and is now DLH PROPERTY SERVICES LTD (event-004) — the customer's recollection, not confirmed with the landlord.
  • That the old account "closes today" and payments there "will bounce" — stated only in the suspect email.

Gaps (per the plan's missing list)

  1. Independent confirmation from the landlord via a contact route the customer already trusted before today.
  2. Whether SYN-SC-08-1 / SYN-ACCT-08-2 is actually closed.
  3. The fraud/security team's assessment.

Contradictions. The payee name the customer was given ("Miss D L Hanlon", personal) contradicts the name and account type held at the receiving bank (business). The email's "going limited" explanation offered by the customer could reconcile this, but the email itself does not say so — it presents the account as "my new account" in a personal voice. The sender/Reply-To mismatch with the historical address cannot be explained from the material supplied. None of this proves the email is fraudulent; it means the bank cannot tell from the email alone, and the plan treats it as a suspected scam pending verification.

3. Why this plan

s1 — Decline override, hold the payee change (skill:fraud-takeover handling rule: suspected scam → hold the specific risky action; do not facilitate on a customer assertion alone). Grounds: cop_result_new_details.json, landlord_email_as_forwarded.eml, customer_previous_landlord_emails.txt, event-004. The CoP result directly contradicts the supplied payee name, and the email carries recognised redirection-scam markers (new sender, different Reply-To, urgency, "old account closes today"). The customer's belief is sincere but unverified. The hold is narrow: only the payee change is held; the existing, long-matched standing order is left untouched and no other banking is affected.

s2 — Urgent fraud/security referral (skill:fraud-takeover handling rule: impersonation/redirection warning signs require an urgent fraud/security handoff alongside the hold). Grounds: event-001, the CoP result, the email headers, the prior-email note. The warning signs are concrete, not speculative, and a loss could occur on the 25th if the change were made, so review is warranted even though no money has moved. The referral packet is limited to facts already in the record; it asks the team to assess and advise on any further protection for the 25th collection, without the plan predicting what they will decide.

s3 — Ask the customer to verify via a trusted route (skill:fraud-takeover handling rule: verify through a known trusted contact, never through details supplied by the suspected source). Grounds: event-004, the email, the prior-email note. The only thing that can safely resolve the mismatch is confirmation from the genuine landlord through a route that pre-dates today's email. Replying to the email, or using its Reply-To, would let the suspect message verify itself.

s4 — Customer reply (general:planner: reply with recorded status, what is on hold, and what has not been confirmed). Grounds: event-003, event-004, the CoP result, the standing order on file. The customer needs the CoP finding, the specific reasons, reassurance that nothing has moved and her rent payment is untouched, a safe verification path, and an honest statement that the outcome depends on the fraud review and the landlord's confirmation. The reply deliberately does not call the email fraudulent, does not say the old account is closed, and does not predict whether the 25th payment will succeed.

Order. s1 and s2 have no dependencies and should proceed first; s4 depends on both so the customer is told about a hold and a referral that have actually been actioned. s3 is the request embedded in the reply.

Alternatives considered within the plan's scope. Overriding CoP on the customer's assertion (event-004) was rejected because the mismatch is to a different legal entity and account type and the supporting explanation is unverified. Changing the payee conditionally "to be safe before the 25th" would move £1,150 to an unverified business account — the exact harm the hold prevents. These are rule sources from the skill, not a supplied bank policy document or a stated legal obligation; no bank policy text or statute was supplied in this case.

4. Execution boundaries

  • s1 and s2 are independent and can be actioned together. s4 requires acknowledgement that s1 and s2 have been executed; the draft wording that refers to the hold as already in place is held in future_messages and labelled NOT SENT until then.
  • The plan stops after s4. It does not decide what happens if the landlord confirms or denies the details, or what the fraud team recommends; those require a fresh decision with the new information. The plan's escalate_if list is empty.
  • No action in this plan alters the existing standing order, blocks the account, sends any payment, or contacts the landlord on the customer's behalf.

5. Expected result and limits

What the proposed steps could achieve: prevent an unverified redirection of £1,150; get a fraud/security assessment before the 25th; give the customer a safe way to resolve the question herself; keep her existing rent payment untouched.

What remains unconfirmed: whether the landlord genuinely trades as DLH PROPERTY SERVICES LTD and holds the new account; whether the old payee account is closed and what will happen to the 25th collection; the fraud team's conclusions and any further protection they recommend.

Constraints carried into the customer message (must_state): CoP result and hold; the specific warning signs; nothing sent and existing order unchanged for the 25th; verify via a pre-existing trusted route only; fraud review is being requested and next steps depend on it and the landlord's answer; the request can be reconsidered on trusted confirmation.

Constraints observed (must_not): no override or payee change on assertion alone; no statement that the email is fraudulent or that the landlord is/is not DLH PROPERTY SERVICES LTD; no claim the old account is closed or the 25th payment will bounce/succeed; no reimbursement promise or review timescale; no verification via the new email or its Reply-To; no PINs, passwords or one-time codes; no claim that the referral or hold is already executed.

Residual uncertainty: the "going limited" explanation is plausible and the plan does not dismiss it; if confirmed through a trusted route it would materially change the picture, but that is for a subsequent decision.

Plan

Intent: Customer asks the bank to override a Confirmation of Payee no-match and redirect her £1,150 rent standing order to new account details supplied in an email from a previously unseen address.
Identity/authority: established

Reason: CoP ANNM — name at bank is DLH PROPERTY SERVICES LTD (business) versus 'Miss D L Hanlon' supplied; new details came from an unfamiliar sender with a different Reply-To and a same-day urgency message. ANSWER.md

Decision

Hold closure today — pending the customer's informed confirmation (disposition: needs_info).

Owen Price (ID&V passed, event-002) has asked for his Harrow & Vale current account to be closed today with the balance sent to his new bank, and for "anything that comes in" to be forwarded (event-004). The account record (event-001, attachments/account_summary.csv) does not match his statement that nothing remains: two active Direct Debits, a standing order and recurring employer credits are still attached, and no CASS switch request is on file. The supplied closure policy permits closure on instruction but requires a failure warning first. The request to manually forward future inbound payments is declined as unsupported — no such facility exists in the supplied policy; only a CASS full switch requested via the new bank redirects payments.

Nothing has been executed. The account is not closed, the £12.40 has not been sent, and no mandates have been cancelled.

Proposed actions (in order)

  1. s1 — Hold / decline (Servicing agent). Hold execution of the closure pending the customer's informed confirmation after the mandatory failure warning. Decline the request to manually forward future inbound credits to another bank (no such facility supplied; CASS full switch is the only redirection route).
  2. s2 — Reply to customer (on the call). Give the recorded status and the required failure warning (script below). Depends on s1.
  3. s3 — Ask customer. Ask for one decision — close today anyway, or keep open until mandates/CASS are arranged — and record the answer verbatim together with the new-bank sort code and account number nominated for the closing balance. Depends on s2. The plan stops here.

Message to the customer (proposed — to be said on the call, not yet delivered)

> Before I close it, our record shows these are still attached to this account: a Direct Debit to Meridian Motor Insurance for £68.20 due 1 October, a Direct Debit to FitLife Gyms for £45.00 due 1 October, a standing order of £200 to S PRICE due 28 September, and your employer's expense reimbursements still come here (last one £83.10 on 14 September). There is no switch request on file. > > If I close the account today, those Direct Debits and the standing order will fail and any expenses your employer sends here will not reach you — I'm required to warn you of that. The insurer letter you forwarded says a missed payment can lead to your policy being cancelled after 14 days unpaid, so please make sure that Direct Debit is set up at your new bank before 1 October. > > I can't set up forwarding of future payments to another bank; the only way to have payments in and out redirected is a full switch through the Current Account Switch Service, which you request from your new bank — it then redirects payments for three years. > > Your current balance is £12.40 and it would be paid to the account you nominate when closure is processed. Nothing has been closed yet. > > Knowing those items will fail, do you want me to close the account today anyway, or keep it open while you set up the insurance and gym Direct Debits, the standing order to your mum and your employer payments at the new bank (or ask the new bank for a full switch), and then close it? And can you give me the sort code and account number you want the closing balance paid to?

What is still needed before anything is executed

  • The customer's informed choice after the warning (close today regardless / hold until re-pointed or CASS requested).
  • The new-bank sort code and account number for the £12.40 closing balance (mentioned on the call, not recorded in evidence).
  • Whether replacement DDs/SO already exist at the new bank (H&V records still show the instructions active).

REASONING.md

REASONING.md — Case 009, request to close a current account

Full input is saved in context.md.

1. Situation and history

All events are dated 2026-09-21 (day precision only; conversation order preserved).

  • event-001 (internal account view, authenticated bank record): Owen Price's H&V personal current account, balance £12.40. Customer opened an account at another bank in August and moved his salary manually; no Current Account Switch Service request on file. Still attached: DD Meridian Motor Insurance £68.20 (next 1 Oct), DD FitLife Gyms £45.00 (next 1 Oct), SO £200 to "S PRICE" on the 28th monthly, and inbound employer expense reimbursements (last 14 Sep, £83.10). The note also records the closure policy supplied for this case: closure with active DDs/SOs is permitted on instruction but the bank must warn they will fail; a CASS full switch (redirecting payments in and out for 3 years) is available at the new bank's request within 7 working days.
  • event-002 (phone, customer, ID&V passed): asks to close today, says everything moved in August, salary now goes to the new bank, "send me whatever's left".
  • event-003 (phone, agent): says it can be arranged but wants to check items still attached.
  • event-004 (phone, customer): asserts "there's nothing on it", instructs closure, and asks that anything that comes in be forwarded to the new account, "here are the details".
  • attachments/account_summary.csv (bank record): confirms the four items above with policy reference MMI-44-88120, dates 2026-10-01 / 2026-10-01 / 2026-09-28, the £12.40 balance and "CASS switch request: none on file".
  • attachments/insurer_reminder_letter.txt (forwarded by the customer; insurer's text dated 15 Sep 2026): policy MMI-44-88120, £68.20 monthly DD on the 1st; if a payment is not received and remains unpaid after 14 days the policy will be cancelled.

Distinction: the customer's account of having "moved everything" (events 002, 004) is his report. The authenticated record (event-001, account_summary.csv) shows active mandates and recurring credits still on the H&V account.

2. Evidence and gaps

  • Identity: ID&V passed on the phone channel (event-002); the plan treats identity authority as established. No safety flags.
  • Contradiction: the customer says nothing remains (event-004); the bank record shows two active DDs, an active SO and recurring inbound credits with no CASS on file (account_summary.csv, event-001). The record is the authoritative source; the customer may have set up duplicate instructions at the new bank, but that cannot be established from the supplied evidence.
  • New-bank details: event-004 says "here are the details" but no sort code or account number is recorded anywhere in the supplied evidence. They cannot be assumed.
  • Insurer letter: this is the insurer's stated term, forwarded by the customer. It is not a bank finding and does not establish whether the policy will in fact be cancelled.
  • Forwarding of future credits: the supplied policy (event-001) describes no facility for H&V to manually forward inbound payments to another bank after closure. The only redirection mechanism supplied is the CASS full switch, requested via the new bank.
  • Not established: whether the customer already has replacement DDs/SO at the new bank; whether the new bank has been asked for a switch; the customer's decision once warned.

3. Why this plan

s1 — Hold / decline (source: skill:cards-access). The supplied closure policy (event-001) allows closure with active DDs/SOs only after the bank warns the customer they will fail. The customer's instruction in event-004 was given on the mistaken premise that nothing remains, so it is not yet an informed instruction. Criterion: closure permitted only after the failure warning; do not claim closure or invent an unsupported operation from a request alone. The manual forwarding request is declined because nothing in the supplied policy supports it — this is a limit of the supplied bank policy, not a legal finding. The planner notes CASS as the only supplied redirection route. Evidence: event-004, account_summary.csv, event-001.

s2 — Reply to customer (source: skill:scheduled-payments). The customer must be told the recorded status and given the required warning so that his closure instruction can be informed. The script lists exactly the items in account_summary.csv (amounts, dates, counterparties), states the consequence of closure (DDs/SO fail, inbound credits not received), relays the insurer's own 14-day term from insurer_reminder_letter.txt without asserting what the insurer will do, explains that forwarding is not available and that CASS is requested via the new bank, states the £12.40 balance would be paid to the nominated account only when closure is processed, and says nothing has been closed. Criterion: state the recorded status and what it does not confirm — closing does not establish that payments were re-pointed. Depends on s1 because the warning follows from the decision to hold.

s3 — Ask customer (source: general:planner). The decisive missing fact is the customer's informed choice after the warning; the second missing input is the nominated account for the closing balance, which is not in evidence. Criterion: request the specific essential missing input and stop; do not schedule closure behind an assumed answer. Depends on s2 — the question only makes sense once the warning has been given.

Order and alternatives. The order (hold → warn → ask) follows the supplied policy's sequence: warning before closure. Closing today on the event-004 instruction alone would skip the mandated warning and would, on the record, cause the insurance DD on 1 Oct to fail. Cancelling the DDs/SO proactively is excluded by the plan (must_not) because the customer has not instructed it. Setting up forwarding is excluded because no such facility is supplied. The plan does not pre-decide what happens if the customer chooses either option; that is a fresh decision on his answer.

4. Execution boundaries

  • Nothing has been executed. The account remains open, the £12.40 has not been remitted, no mandates have been cancelled, and no CASS request has been or can be initiated by H&V.
  • s2 requires s1; s3 requires s2. Execution stops at s3 pending: (a) the customer's verbatim choice, (b) the new-bank sort code and account number for the closing balance, and (c) whether replacement instructions already exist at the new bank.
  • If the customer's answer or the nominated details are not obtained on the call, closure stays held; a new decision is required rather than proceeding on assumption.
  • The plan contains no conditional branches and none are added here.

5. Expected result and limits

What the steps can achieve: an informed closure instruction (or an informed decision to hold), a recorded nomination of the destination account for the balance, and compliance with the supplied warning requirement before any closure. The customer is alerted to the 1 Oct insurance collection and the insurer's stated 14-day cancellation term in time to act.

What remains unconfirmed: whether the customer will choose to close today; whether the new bank already holds replacement mandates; whether a CASS switch will be requested, when, or with what outcome (that is the new bank's process, within the supplied "7 working days" availability statement, and H&V cannot promise it); whether the insurer would actually cancel — only the insurer's stated term is relayed.

Constraints preserved (must_state / must_not): the reply states all four attached items with amounts and dates, the failure warning, the insurer's term, the unavailability of manual forwarding and the CASS route, that closure is not yet actioned, and that the £12.40 is paid only when closure is processed. It does not state or imply closure or remittance has happened, does not promise forwarding, does not predict the insurer's decision, does not promise CASS timing or outcome, does not cancel any mandate, and does not request PINs, passwords or one-time codes.

ANSWER.md

Decision

Refused (proposed disposition). The caller, Dave Tilley of Tilley's Maintenance, is not a Harrow & Vale customer and holds no recorded mandate or authorisation from Haldane Homes Ltd. All three requests — which invoice the £90 payment relates to, whether Haldane Homes has funds to pay his £1,400 invoice, and a direct number for whoever looks after Haldane Homes's account — would disclose another customer's account information to an unauthorised third party and are to be declined. No bank-system action or escalation is proposed; the caller is to be given general information only.

Proposed actions (in order, none executed)

  1. s1 — Confirm identity/authority (record only). Record that the caller is a non-customer (banks with Meridian) with no recorded mandate or authorisation from Haldane Homes Ltd. No identification check is to be attempted for the purpose of releasing Haldane Homes's information, because no supported check could establish that authority.
  2. s2 — Hold / decline disclosure. Decline to disclose any Haldane Homes Ltd account information: the invoice allocation of the £90 payment, balance or ability to pay the £1,400 invoice, payment history, and any direct contact for the bank's handling of Haldane Homes's account. Reason: third party without the customer's authority.
  3. s3 — Reply to the caller (after s2). Deliver the message below. Do not confirm or deny any account details.

Proposed message to the caller

> I understand the reference isn't helpful, but I'm not able to discuss another customer's account with anyone other than that customer or someone they've authorised — that includes what a payment relates to, whether there are funds to pay an invoice, or who looks after their account here. The reference on a payment is typed in by the payer, so the best route is Haldane Homes's accounts payable team: ask them for a remittance advice for the £90 payment of 1 September and for an update on your £1,400 invoice. Your own bank, Meridian, can show you the full details of the credit you received. If Haldane Homes needs our help with the payment, they can contact us directly through their business banking channels.

Boundaries

  • Do not confirm or deny Haldane Homes's balance, available funds, ability to pay, or any payment or account activity.
  • Do not disclose which invoice the payment was for or anything beyond what the caller already holds from his own credit.
  • Do not provide any name, number or contact details for a relationship manager or team handling Haldane Homes's account.
  • Do not suggest the caller can obtain this by supplying further details, or that a future disclosure will be made.
  • Do not imply the caller is doing anything wrong or allege fraud.

REASONING.md

Decision memo — Case 010: Question about a payment reference

1. Situation and history

All four events are dated 2026-09-22 on the business banking phone line; no individual times are recorded. There are no attachments (see context.md, request.md).

  • event-001 (internal account view, bank case record): The caller identifies himself as Dave Tilley, Tilley's Maintenance, not an H&V customer. He reports receiving £90.00 on 1 Sep from "HALDANE HOMES LTD" with reference HALDANE 281475554136064010F274262443HS9608382 01SEP26. The note also records that Haldane Homes Ltd is an H&V business customer and that the agent can see, in Haldane Homes's business account, a £90.00 Faster Payment to payee "TILLEYS MAINTENANCE" on 1 Sep plus other payments to the same payee in Aug/Sep.
  • event-002 (caller, phone): Three requests: (a) which invoice the £90 payment relates to; (b) whether Haldane Homes has the money in the account to pay his overdue £1,400 invoice; (c) a direct number for whoever looks after Haldane Homes's account at the bank.
  • event-003 (agent, phone): Asks whether the caller holds an H&V account.
  • event-004 (caller, phone): Confirms he does not; he banks with Meridian, and argues the bank "must be able to see it" because its customer paid him.

Distinction of sources. The caller's identity, his subcontractor relationship with Haldane Homes, the £90 receipt and the £1,400 overdue invoice are the caller's own report (event-002, event-004). The authenticated bank record is the internal view in event-001: Haldane Homes Ltd's customer status and the outgoing payments to "TILLEYS MAINTENANCE". That internal view is bank-held information about Haldane Homes, not information the caller is entitled to receive.

2. Evidence and gaps

  • No authority to receive Haldane Homes's information. Nothing in event-001, event-002 or event-004 records a mandate, authorisation or consent from Haldane Homes Ltd permitting disclosure to Tilley's Maintenance. The plan's missing entry records this explicitly; the supported route is Haldane Homes contacting the bank itself. This is the controlling gap and it cannot be closed by anything the caller could say or supply on this call.
  • The invoice allocation cannot be established from bank records. The payment reference was keyed by the payer. Even setting privacy aside, the bank's record (event-001) does not show which invoice the £90 relates to; only Haldane Homes's accounts payable can say.
  • Affordability ("have they got the money?") is a direct balance enquiry on another customer's account (event-002). There is no basis on which this could be answered to a third party.
  • Contact details for the bank's handling of Haldane Homes's account would expose the customer's relationship contact to an unauthorised party (event-002).
  • The caller's argument in event-004 ("it's your customer that paid me, so you must be able to see it") is accurate as to visibility but not as to entitlement: the agent can see the payment, which is precisely why the plan's step s2 notes it must not be shared.
  • No indication of fraud or wrongdoing by the caller exists in the record; the request appears to be an ordinary payee trying to reconcile a payment. The plan's must_not forbids implying otherwise.

3. Why this plan

s1 — confirm_identity_authority (record only). *Explanation:* the caller has confirmed he banks with Meridian and is asking about a different customer's account; nothing gives him authority over that account, and no supported check could create it. *Evidence:* event-001, event-004. *Criterion:* disclosure of account information requires the account holder's established identity or an authorised third-party mandate; a payee relationship is not authority. *Source:* the privacy-documents skill handling rule (not a supplied bank policy document or cited statute). The practical consequence is that no ID verification is attempted for the purpose of releasing Haldane Homes's data — doing so would wrongly imply verification could unlock it, which must_not prohibits.

s2 — hold_or_decline. *Explanation:* each of the three requests would reveal a customer's private financial information or expose the customer's bank contact to an unauthorised party; the bank's internal view cannot be shared merely because the agent can see it. *Evidence:* event-001, event-002, event-004. *Criterion:* hold the unsafe disclosure where there is a privacy/authority gap; limit disclosure to the request and established authority. *Source:* privacy-documents skill handling rule. The plan's identity_authority: not_established and safety flags privacy and identity_authority drive this step.

s3 — reply_to_customer (requires s2). *Explanation:* gives the caller the useful general information that is available — the reference is payer-set; Haldane Homes's accounts payable is the right contact for a remittance advice on the £90 of 1 Sep and an update on the £1,400 invoice; his own bank (Meridian) can show the full details of the credit he received; Haldane Homes can contact H&V itself through its business banking channels — while stating clearly what cannot be disclosed and why. *Evidence:* event-002, event-004, request.md. *Criterion:* hold only the unsafe disclosure, identify the supported route and still provide useful general information. *Source:* general safety handling (general:safety), not a supplied bank policy. The message wording is lifted from the plan and contains no confirmation or denial of any account detail. Note that the message's references to "£90 payment of 1 September" and "£1,400 invoice" are the caller's own figures from event-002, not bank data.

Ordering. s1 and s2 have no dependencies; s3 depends on s2 so that the refusal position is fixed before anything is said to the caller. Nothing is dependent on any bank-system operation.

Alternatives considered within the plan's scope. The only supported alternative route is Haldane Homes Ltd contacting H&V directly, which the message points to. Escalation is not proposed (escalate_if is empty) because there is no fraud indicator, no dispute on an H&V account, and no system action requested. Partial disclosure (e.g. "yes, we can see that payment") is excluded by must_not — confirming visibility would itself confirm Haldane Homes's account activity.

4. Execution boundaries

  • No action has been taken. s1 is an internal record of the authority position; s2 is the decision to decline; s3 is the spoken reply, pending delivery.
  • No bank-system operation, case registration, callback, escalation or handoff is proposed.
  • The plan stops at the reply. If Haldane Homes Ltd itself contacts the bank with its own authentication, that would be a new contact requiring its own decision; the plan does not pre-decide it, and the caller must not be told that any future disclosure will be made.
  • If the caller presses with further details (invoice numbers, company registration, etc.), the refusal stands — the gap is authority, not information, and the plan does not include any route for the caller to cure it.

5. Expected result and limits

Achievable: the caller leaves with a clear, non-accusatory explanation of why the bank cannot help with Haldane Homes's account, and with practical next steps (Haldane Homes accounts payable for remittance advice and invoice status; Meridian for his own credit details; Haldane Homes contacting H&V if it needs bank help with the reference). All four must_state points are covered in the s3 message.

Not achieved / unconfirmed: the caller will not learn which invoice the £90 payment covers, whether Haldane Homes can pay the £1,400 invoice, or who handles Haldane Homes's account at H&V — by design. The bank cannot resolve his reconciliation or credit-control problem; only Haldane Homes can. Whether Haldane Homes will provide a remittance advice or settle the £1,400 invoice is outside the bank's control and not promised.

Constraints preserved: no confirmation or denial of balance, funds, ability to pay, or account activity; no invoice allocation or account details beyond what the caller holds from his own credit; no relationship manager or team contact details; no suggestion that further details would unlock disclosure; no implication of wrongdoing or fraud. Expected disposition: refused.

Plan

Intent: Third-party (non-customer) caller asks the bank to explain a payment reference, disclose whether an H&V business customer (Haldane Homes Ltd) has funds to pay his £1,400 invoice, and provide a direct number for that customer's account contact.
Identity/authority: not_established
