ANSWER.md

ANSWER — Case 011: Refund of duplicate £1,096.00 credit (Paul Devlin t/a Devlin Plumbing)

Decision

Proceed with the refund as a return to source, subject to two gates. The refund details the client supplied (sort code SYN-SC-11-2, account SYN-ACCT-11-2) are identical to the originating account of both £1,096.00 Faster Payment credits, and Confirmation of Payee on those details returned a match to "M KOWALCZYK". The "A KOWALCZYK" / "M Kowalczyk" name difference is unverified by the bank but does not block a return to the originating account. Nothing is to be sent until (1) the bank's records have been checked for an existing Meridian recall on either credit and (2) Paul confirms the payment in the secure business banking channel.

Disposition: needs_info (pending the recall check and Paul's secure confirmation).

Proposed actions (in order — none executed yet)

  1. s1 — Check records (pending). Check Harrow & Vale Bank's inbound Faster Payment / recall-and-return records for SYN-SC-11-1 / SYN-ACCT-11-1 for any recall, return request or indemnity claim from Meridian Bank relating to end-to-end IDs MER26092109401201 (21 Sep, £1,096.00) or MER26092214204811 (22 Sep, £1,096.00). Record the result on case 011 before any outbound payment is released.
  1. s2 — Set up payment for secure confirmation (pending; depends on s1). Prepare one outbound Faster Payment of £1,096.00 from SYN-SC-11-1 / SYN-ACCT-11-1 to sort code SYN-SC-11-2, account SYN-ACCT-11-2, payee name "M Kowalczyk" (CoP matched "M KOWALCZYK" at 2026-09-23T10:05:33Z), reference "REFUND INV 2211 DUPLICATE", for Paul Devlin's confirmation in the business banking channel. Note on the case that this returns the second credit (end-to-end ID MER26092214204811) to its originating account. Release only on Paul's confirmation and only if s1 shows no existing Meridian recall for that credit.
  1. s3 — Reply to customer (pending; depends on s1 and s2). Send the message below in the business banking chat.

Proposed message to the customer (not yet sent)

> Thanks — that helps. The key point is that the details your client gave (SYN-SC-11-2 / SYN-ACCT-11-2) are the same sort code and account number both £1,096 payments came from, and Confirmation of Payee matches 'M KOWALCZYK' on that account. So this would be a return to the account that paid you, not a payment to a new destination. We can't verify who holds that account or confirm the relationship you've described — the payer name on the credits shows 'A KOWALCZYK' — but because it's the originating account, that doesn't block the refund. Nothing has been sent yet. I'm first checking whether the payer's bank has already asked us to return one of the payments, so you don't end up refunding twice. I'll then send you a £1,096.00 payment to those details with reference 'REFUND INV 2211 DUPLICATE' to confirm securely in your business banking; it only goes once you confirm. I can't promise an arrival time. Please keep your client's text and these payment IDs (MER26092109401201, MER26092214204811) for your records.

What this does not do

  • It does not treat the forwarded text as a verified instruction from the payer, and it does not assert that the Meridian account is joint, is Marek's, or that Anna is authorised on it.
  • It does not state that no Meridian recall exists — only that none is recorded in the supplied snapshot; the s1 check is what establishes this.
  • It does not request any PIN, password or one-time code in chat; confirmation happens in the secure business banking channel.

REASONING.md

REASONING — Case 011: Refund of duplicate £1,096.00 credit

Full input is in the saved context.md. All actions below are proposed; none has been executed.

1. Situation and history

Customer: Paul Devlin t/a Devlin Plumbing, sole-trader business account SYN-SC-11-1 / SYN-ACCT-11-1 at Harrow & Vale Bank plc. Contact is via the bank's business banking chat on 2026-09-23 (individual event times not recorded; events follow conversation order).

Authenticated bank records (event-001, attachments/inbound_payments.json):

  • 21 Sep 2026 09:40 BST — Faster Payment credit +£1,096.00, payer name text "A KOWALCZYK", Meridian Bank SYN-SC-11-2 / SYN-ACCT-11-2, reference "INV 2211 BOILER", end-to-end ID MER26092109401201.
  • 22 Sep 2026 14:20 BST — Faster Payment credit +£1,096.00, same payer name text, same payer sort code/account, same reference, end-to-end ID MER26092214204811.
  • Confirmation of Payee (attachments/cop_result_refund_details.json) on SYN-SC-11-2 / SYN-ACCT-11-2, name supplied "M Kowalczyk", account type personal: matched: true, name at bank "M KOWALCZYK", checked 2026-09-23T10:05:33Z.

Customer's report (event-002, event-004, request.md): Paul states his customer paid invoice 2211 twice and has texted him asking for one payment to be returned to "her husband's account" SYN-ACCT-11-2 / SYN-SC-11-2, M Kowalczyk, "that's what I paid from". When the agent queried the A/M name difference (event-003), Paul said Anna is the client, Marek is her husband, the money is joint, the account is Marek's and she uses it; he wants to refund but not to the wrong place.

Forwarded third-party message (attachments/client_text_message.txt): text dated Tue 22 Sep 19:02 from a number saved as "Anna K - boiler job", attributing the duplicate to an app glitch and asking for one payment back to the same details. This is a forwarded message supplied by Paul, not an instruction verified by the bank.

2. Evidence and gaps

Established from authenticated records:

  • Two credits of identical amount, reference and source account one day apart, with distinct end-to-end IDs — consistent with a duplicate payment (event-001, inbound_payments.json).
  • The refund destination (SYN-SC-11-2 / SYN-ACCT-11-2) is character-for-character the originating sort code and account number of both credits (inbound_payments.json vs event-002 and client_text_message.txt).
  • CoP matches "M KOWALCZYK" on that account (cop_result_refund_details.json).

Observed discrepancy: the inbound payer name text is "A KOWALCZYK" while the account name at the receiving bank is "M KOWALCZYK" (event-003). Paul's explanation (event-004) is a customer assertion the bank cannot verify. It is not contradicted by the records, but it is also not corroborated by them: the payer-name text in a Faster Payment message is not a verified account-holder name, and this bank has no view of the Meridian account's holders or mandate.

Gaps (from the plan's missing list):

  1. Whether Meridian Bank has already lodged a recall or return request for either end-to-end ID. The supplied snapshot records none, but this is an absence of record in the snapshot, not a negative finding — the payer said the app "glitched" and may also have contacted her own bank.
  2. Paul's secure confirmation of the outbound £1,096.00 payment — not yet given; the chat exchange is a request, not an authorised payment instruction.
  3. Independent evidence of who holds the Meridian account or of the Anna/Marek relationship — not obtainable by this bank, and per the plan not required, because the payment goes back to the account the funds came from rather than to a new destination.

Identity: the plan records identity authority as established (account holder in the bank's business banking chat about his own account). No separate verification record is supplied; this is noted as a limit rather than a contradiction.

3. Why this plan

Core judgement (plan summary): the risk Paul raised — "sending it to the wrong place" — is addressed not by resolving the name difference but by the fact that the destination is the exact originating account, confirmed by CoP. The A/M difference is therefore unverified but immaterial to a return-to-source. Had the client asked for the refund to go to any *different* account, the same evidence would not support proceeding; that alternative is not the case here and is not part of the decision.

s1 — check recall/return records first.

  • *Explanation:* if Meridian has already lodged a recall, a customer-initiated refund would return the same £1,096.00 twice.
  • *Evidence:* inbound_payments.json (the two end-to-end IDs), client_text_message.txt (the "app glitched" account that could prompt a recall at the payer's bank), event-001 (snapshot shows no recall recorded).
  • *Criterion:* a named journal lookup is justified only for a material gap; a recall/return request is distinct from a completed return and must not be duplicated.
  • *Source:* skill:payments handling rule (not a supplied bank policy or legal obligation).

s2 — set up the payment for Paul's secure confirmation (after s1).

  • *Explanation:* Paul is the account holder instructing a payment from his own account; the destination is the originating account and CoP matches. The payment is state-changing and requires his secure authorisation; it is also gated on s1 showing no existing Meridian recall for the credit being returned (MER26092214204811, the second credit).
  • *Evidence:* event-002 (request), event-004 (confirmation of intent), inbound_payments.json, cop_result_refund_details.json.
  • *Criterion:* identify the exact transaction by reference, amount, date and parties; a customer-authorised outbound payment requires the customer's secure confirmation before release.
  • *Source:* skill:payments handling rule.

s3 — reply to the customer (after s1 and s2).

  • *Explanation:* answers the concern with the evidenced basis (same source account, CoP match), labels the unverified assertions as such, states that nothing has been sent and that release depends on the recall check and his confirmation, and avoids any arrival-time promise.
  • *Evidence:* event-003, event-004, cop_result_refund_details.json, inbound_payments.json.
  • *Criterion:* state only the stage the supplied record proves; do not promise arrival times absent an applicable commitment.
  • *Source:* skill:payments handling rule.

Ordering: s1 precedes s2 because the recall check determines whether the refund should be released at all; s2 precedes s3 so the message accurately describes a payment that is set up and awaiting confirmation rather than one that is still hypothetical. The message text in s3 is nonetheless written so that it remains truthful if read before s2 is complete ("Nothing has been sent yet… I'll then send you a payment… to confirm").

Safety flags: none raised in the plan. The refund is to the originating account, which removes the main redirection risk; CoP is a match, not a near-match or mismatch.

4. Execution boundaries

  • s1 has no dependencies and can proceed immediately. Its result must be recorded on case 011 before anything else is released.
  • s2 requires s1. Release of the payment is additionally conditional on (a) s1 showing no existing Meridian recall for MER26092214204811 and (b) Paul's secure confirmation in the business banking channel. If s1 shows a recall or return request already lodged, the plan does not authorise release; that would require a fresh decision, not an alternative devised here.
  • s3 requires s1 and s2 and is the only customer-facing communication in the plan.
  • The plan stops at Paul's confirmation. It does not pre-decide what happens if he declines, if CoP changes, or if Meridian contacts the bank later. No escalation conditions are set (escalate_if is empty).

5. Expected result and limits

What the steps could achieve: confirmation that no double return is in train; a £1,096.00 Faster Payment to SYN-SC-11-2 / SYN-ACCT-11-2 with reference "REFUND INV 2211 DUPLICATE" queued for Paul's secure authorisation; and a clear explanation to Paul of why the refund can proceed despite the name difference and what the bank cannot verify.

What remains unconfirmed: whether a Meridian recall exists (until s1 is run); whether Paul will confirm; who holds the Meridian account and whether Anna is authorised on it (not verifiable by this bank); arrival timing (no commitment given).

Constraints preserved in the message (must_state): same source account → return to source; CoP match to "M KOWALCZYK"; inbound payer name "A KOWALCZYK" and the bank's inability to verify the holder/relationship, with the originating-account basis made explicit; nothing sent yet and release only on secure confirmation; recall check in progress and no arrival-time promise; suggested reference and advice to retain the text and end-to-end IDs.

Constraints observed (must_not): the message does not say the account is joint, Marek's, or that Anna is authorised; does not say the refund has been sent, credited or will arrive by a time; does not claim the name difference is verified or resolved; discloses nothing about the Meridian account beyond the CoP match already shown; does not treat the forwarded text as a verified payer instruction; does not request a PIN, password or one-time code; and does not state that no recall exists — only that the bank is checking.

Note on plan sufficiency: the plan is adequate for the facts supplied. The one point an operator should hold in mind is that the snapshot's silence on recalls is not evidence of their absence, which is exactly why s1 is placed first and gates s2.

ANSWER.md

Decision

Decline the release request and keep bulk payment batch 1709 (£28,410.00, Kestrel Joinery Ltd, account SYN-ACCT-12-1) on hold; escalate urgently to fraud/security. Expected disposition: escalated.

The approval recorded at 15:22:04 under m.kestrel was, by Claire's own account (event-004), entered by claire.b (permissions: view; payments_create only) from the same device fingerprint (7f3a91c0) and IP that created the batch. No independent second authoriser has approved the batch, so the account's dual-authorisation requirement is not met. The director's credentials are also exposed through sharing. Nothing has been executed; every step below is proposed.

Proposed actions (in order)

  1. s1 — Hold / decline (payments control / servicing): Keep batch 1709 on hold and decline release. Scope is this batch only; no other account activity is held by this step.
  2. s2 — Urgent escalation (fraud/security team), after s1: Refer that m.kestrel credentials were shared with and used by claire.b on 2026-09-17 (device 7f3a91c0, IP 81.xx.xx.14) to approve batch 1709 while Mark Kestrel is noted on leave 15–25 Sep. Ask the team to (a) secure the m.kestrel credentials pending reset via the bank's secure route, (b) rule on whether batch 1709 must be cancelled and recreated or can receive a fresh legitimate approval, and (c) if contact with Mark is needed, use only bank-held recorded contact details, not a number supplied in chat. Pass attachments/batch_1709_audit.csv and attachments/mandate_users.csv.
  3. s3 — Route to the secure process (to Claire), after s2: Explain the supported route: a genuine second approver must approve under their own credentials on their own device. The mandate shows Ruth Kestrel (r.kestrel, Director, payments_approve, active) can do this, subject to the security team's ruling on whether the batch must be recreated. Mark may only approve himself, from his own device, after his credentials have been reset through the bank's secure route.
  4. s4 — Reply to Claire, after s1–s3: Send the message below. It must not state that the security referral has already been actioned.

Message to the customer (proposed, not yet sent)

> I can't release batch 1709. The approval recorded at 15:22 under Mark's login was entered by you from the same device that created the batch, so the account's dual-authorisation requirement hasn't been met — a shared login doesn't count as Mark approving, even if he agreed. Sharing a login is also a security issue, so I'm referring Mark's credentials to our security team; he'll need to reset them through our secure process, and we'd only contact him on details we already hold, not a number given here. The batch stays on hold. The quickest supported route is for Ruth Kestrel, who is an active approver on the mandate, to log in with her own credentials on her own device and approve it; our security team will confirm whether the existing batch can be approved or needs recreating. I can't guarantee it will clear today's 16:30 cut-off. Please never share or enter anyone else's password or codes.

What this does not do

  • Does not release, re-approve, split or otherwise work around the hold on batch 1709.
  • Does not treat Claire's statement of Mark's consent as a recorded authorisation, and does not assert fraud or account takeover as fact.
  • Does not ask for or accept any password, PIN or one-time code.
  • Does not use a phone number offered in chat as a verification channel.
  • Does not promise a call time, a release time, or that suppliers will be paid today.

REASONING.md

Decision memo — Case 012, Kestrel Joinery Ltd, batch 1709

Full input is saved in context.md. All actions are proposals; no bank tools are connected and nothing has been executed.

1. Situation and history

Authenticated bank records (event-001, attachments/batch_1709_audit.csv, attachments/mandate_users.csv):

  • Kestrel Joinery Ltd holds a business current account (SYN-SC-12-1 / SYN-ACCT-12-1) with dual authorisation required for bulk payments.
  • 2026-09-17 15:20:11 — batch 1709 (12 supplier payments, £28,410.00) created by claire.b, role payments_create, device fingerprint 7f3a91c0, IP 81.xx.xx.14.
  • 2026-09-17 15:22:04 — batch approved under m.kestrel, role payments_approve, same fingerprint 7f3a91c0 and same IP.
  • 2026-09-17 15:22:05 — system control hold: creator and approver on the same device fingerprint.
  • Mandate: claire.b (Claire Bishop, Bookkeeper) has view; payments_create only. m.kestrel (Mark Kestrel, Director) and r.kestrel (Ruth Kestrel, Director) both hold payments_approve and mandate_admin; both active. r.kestrel last login 2026-09-12 09:40. m.kestrel last login 2026-09-17 15:21, with a note on file (14 Sep) that he is on annual leave 15–25 Sep.
  • Faster Payments bulk cut-off is 16:30. Contact started 15:35 (request.md); individual message times are not recorded.

Customer's report (business banking chat):

  • event-002: Claire asks for the batch to be released, says "Mark approved it", cites two suppliers threatening to stop deliveries and the 4:30 cut-off.
  • event-003: the agent asks how the approval was done.
  • event-004: Claire states Mark is on holiday, gave her his login this morning, that he "authorised every line" and it was "just me pressing the button for him", and that he is happy to be called.

2. Evidence and gaps

  • The approval was not made by the credential holder. The audit CSV shows identical device fingerprint and IP for creation and approval two minutes apart, and Claire's own statement (event-004) confirms she entered the approval using Mark's login. These two sources are consistent; there is no contradiction between the records and the customer's account on this point.
  • Mark's consent is unevidenced by the bank. The only basis for "Mark authorised every line" is Claire's assertion (event-004). No bank record, message, or verified contact from Mark exists in the supplied context. The plan treats this as an assertion, not an authorisation.
  • Credential exposure is established, takeover is not. Claire's admission establishes that m.kestrel credentials have been shared. Whether that sharing was consensual is not something the bank can verify from the context; the plan therefore does not assert fraud or account takeover as fact, only that the credentials are exposed and the approval record misrepresents who acted.
  • No bank-held contact details for Mark are supplied. Claire offers to have him called, but a number provided in chat by the person who used his login cannot serve as an independent verification channel. Whether the bank holds recorded contact details for Mark is not visible here.
  • No security ruling exists yet on whether batch 1709 can receive a fresh legitimate approval as-is or must be cancelled and recreated.
  • Timing is approximate. Only the 15:35 contact start is recorded; the time remaining before the 16:30 cut-off cannot be stated precisely.

3. Why this plan

s1 — Hold and decline (source: skill business-authority). Dual authorisation requires two distinct authorised users. The audit trail (event-001, audit CSV) and Claire's admission (event-004) show one person acted as both creator and approver using a shared login; the mandate CSV confirms claire.b has no approval permission. The handling rule applied is that an uploader/bookkeeper is not an authorised signatory for approval and a shared credential does not transfer that authority. This is the skill's rule rather than a quoted bank policy clause, but it aligns with the account's recorded dual-authorisation requirement (event-001) and with the system's own control hold. Scope is limited to batch 1709; the plan does not freeze other account activity.

s2 — Urgent fraud/security escalation (source: skill fraud-takeover), requires s1. Credentials of a director with payments_approve and mandate_admin rights are confirmed exposed and were used to generate an approval record that does not reflect who acted, while the holder is recorded as on leave. The rule applied: credential exposure is concrete warning evidence — stop the risky action, hand off urgently to fraud/security, use only known trusted contact routes, and do not assert takeover as fact. The three asks (secure/reset the credentials, rule on batch 1709's status, contact Mark only via bank-held details) follow directly from the gaps in section 2. The escalation comes after s1 so that the batch is definitively held before any security work begins. The audit and mandate CSVs are passed as the evidentiary basis.

s3 — Route to the secure process (source: skill business-authority), requires s2. The mandate CSV shows an unaffected, active second approver, Ruth Kestrel (r.kestrel), so a legitimate route exists without bypassing controls. The rule applied: propose the specific permitted action once authority is properly established; do not split or bypass joint approval limits. The route is expressly subject to the security team's ruling (s2) on whether the batch must be recreated, which is why s3 depends on s2. Mark's own future approval is conditioned on a credential reset through the bank's secure route, on his own device.

s4 — Reply to Claire (source: planner), requires s1–s3. The reply gives the recorded hold status, the reason, the security referral as pending, the legitimate route, and what is not confirmed (cut-off, timing). It makes no promise of outcome and does not facilitate the invalid release. It also instructs Claire never to share or enter another person's password or codes.

Alternatives considered within the plan's scope. Calling Mark on the number Claire offers was rejected: the number comes from the same person who used his login, so it cannot independently verify his consent (plan missing, s2(c)). Releasing on the existing approval was rejected because the approval is not an independent second authorisation. Splitting the batch below any threshold was excluded by the plan's must_not. No further alternatives are added here.

4. Execution boundaries

  • Order and dependencies: s1 → s2 → s3 → s4. s2 requires s1 acknowledged; s3 requires s2; s4 requires s1, s2 and s3.
  • The plan stops at the escalation and the customer reply. It does not decide whether batch 1709 will be approved as-is or recreated — that is the security team's ruling. It does not initiate any approval by Ruth Kestrel; it only identifies her as the mandate-recorded route. It does not reset Mark's credentials; it refers them for securing.
  • If any step cannot be executed (for example, the security team cannot be reached before the cut-off, or the batch status changes), a fresh decision is needed. No branches beyond those in the plan are created here.

5. Expected result and limits

What the proposed steps could achieve: batch 1709 stays held and is not released on a tainted approval; the exposed m.kestrel credentials are referred for securing and reset; a security ruling on the batch is requested; Claire is told the only supported route (a genuine approver on their own credentials and device) and that a chat-supplied number for Mark is not an approval channel.

What remains unconfirmed: whether Mark actually consented; whether the batch can be approved as-is or must be recreated; whether Ruth Kestrel is available and will approve; whether anything can clear before the 16:30 cut-off; whether the security referral has been actioned (it has not — it is pending). The reply must not imply any payment has been released or that the referral is already in hand.

Constraints preserved: all six must_state points appear in the proposed reply (hold and reason; shared-login security issue and reset via secure route; chat-supplied number is not an approval route; Ruth Kestrel as an active approver subject to security review; no guarantee on the 16:30 cut-off; nothing confirms release or that the referral is actioned). All must_not items are respected: no release or re-approval, no request for credentials or codes, Claire's statement is not treated as recorded authorisation and no fraud is asserted as fact, no batch-splitting or workaround, no promised call/release time or supplier payment today, and no use of a Claire-supplied phone number.

1. Situation and history

  • event-001 (2026-09-23, internal account view, Harrow & Vale Bank plc): authenticated bank record for Rina Patel's personal current account.   • attachments/statement_extract.csv: bank statement lines consistent with event-001 — 18 Sep DD RETURNED (balance £710.40); 20 Sep salary credit £2,145.00 (balance £2,855.40); 21 Sep DD −£750.00 marked "(re-presentation)", ref NL-9LIME-0442 (balance £2,105.40).

  • event-002 (2026-09-23, in-app chat, customer's report): Rina received the letter, can see £750 leaving on 21 Sep, and has a text from "the agency" saying the payment failed and asking for a manual payment today. She wants to avoid arrears and avoid paying twice.
  • attachments/agency_text_screenshot.png.ocr.txt: OCR of the text, attributed to "Northgate Lettings", dated Tue 22 Sep 16:20, saying the September Direct Debit "FAILED" and requesting a manual £750 payment to their client account, ref NL-9LIME-0442.
  • event-003 (2026-09-23, agent): "Let me check both dates on the mandate." — the agent's last message, before any answer was given.

Distinction: event-001, the statement extract and the 19 Sep letter are the bank's own records. Event-002 and the text screenshot are the customer's report and a third-party message respectively; the text's origin is not verified by any bank record.

2. Evidence and gaps

Established by bank records

  • The 18 Sep return and its cause (available balance £710.40 < £750), no fee: event-001, statement_extract.csv, unpaid_dd_letter_2026-09-19.pdf.
  • The 19 Sep letter relates to the 18 Sep attempt: the letter itself names "Date presented: 18 September 2026".
  • The 21 Sep re-presentation was paid and £750 debited, ref NL-9LIME-0442: event-001, statement_extract.csv.
  • Mandate active, next collection not before 18 Oct: event-001.

Not established (the plan's missing items)

  • Whether Northgate Lettings has received and reconciled the 21 Sep collection. The bank's records show the debit from Rina's account only; they say nothing about the recipient's credit or the agency's ledger.
  • Whether the 22 Sep text genuinely came from Northgate. The reference NL-9LIME-0442 matches the mandate reference on the statement, which is consistent with a genuine but stale system message, but it is not proof of either genuineness or impersonation. The agency's text post-dates the 21 Sep payment, so a plausible explanation is that their system still reflects the 18 Sep failure — but that is inference, not record.

Apparent contradiction explained: the customer sees "failed" (letter + text) and "paid" (statement) for the same rent. These are two separate presentations of one collection: the first (18 Sep) failed, the second (21 Sep) succeeded. There is no contradiction in the bank's records.

3. Why this plan

The plan has one step, s1 — reply_to_customer, with no dependencies.

  • Explanation (plan rationale): the internal account view already contains both mandate dates, so the lookup the agent proposed in event-003 is already satisfied by event-001; the question can be answered now. The reply must state only what the bank's record proves (the 21 Sep debit) and must not assert that the agency has reconciled it, and must steer the customer away from paying on the strength of an unverified text.
  • Evidence cited by the plan: event-001, event-002, statement_extract.csv, unpaid_dd_letter_2026-09-19.pdf, agency_text_screenshot.png.ocr.txt.
  • Decision criterion: state only the payment stage the supplied record proves (paid on re-presentation, not recipient credit); an active mandate or a letter does not establish the later collection's outcome; a third party's message is not evidence of a booked or missing payment; unexpected payment requests should be verified through a known trusted contact route.
  • Rule source: skill:scheduled-payments — this is the skill's handling rule, not a supplied bank policy document or a legal obligation. No bank policy or statutory text was supplied in the case materials, and none is cited here.

Why no bank-side action: the mandate behaved correctly (returned for insufficient funds, then re-presented and paid); no fee was charged; the next collection is not before 18 Oct. Nothing indicates a mandate error, so cancelling or amending the mandate would not be supported by the evidence (must_not). The customer has not asked the bank to send any payment (event-002, request.md).

Why advise against the manual payment now: paying would create the double-payment risk the customer wants to avoid, and the only basis for the request is a message whose authenticity and currency are unverified. The safe route is confirmation with Northgate via details she already holds (tenancy agreement / official website), not the text's contact details (must_not: do not quote the text's contact details as trusted). If Northgate deny sending the text, the plan asks her not to pay and to report back to the bank as a possible impersonation; the plan does not go further than that.

Alternatives considered within the plan's bounds: a further internal lookup (as event-003 suggested) adds nothing because event-001 already covers both dates. Treating the text as definitely a scam or definitely genuine is not supported either way, so the message states neither.

4. Execution boundaries

  • Only s1 is proposed; it has no requires and no condition. It is pending until sent.
  • The plan stops after the reply. It does not commit the bank to any action on the agency's side, does not open a fraud/impersonation case (that would only arise if the customer returns with Northgate's denial, which the plan treats as a report-back, not a pre-authorised step), and does not propose any Direct Debit Guarantee or refund route.
  • If the customer returns with new information (e.g. Northgate disown the text, or say they have not received the 21 Sep funds), that requires a fresh decision; no branch is pre-decided here.
  • escalate_if is empty and safety_flags is empty; identity authority is established, so the in-app reply can be given.

5. Expected result and limits

What s1 can achieve: gives the customer a clear, record-based account of the 18/19/21 Sep sequence; reassures her that, from the bank's side, September's rent has been collected and no further collection is due before 18 Oct; prevents a premature duplicate payment; directs her to verify the text through a trusted route. Expected disposition: completed.

What remains unconfirmed: whether Northgate has received/reconciled the 21 Sep payment, and whether the 22 Sep text is genuine. Both can only be resolved by the customer's contact with Northgate; the bank cannot confirm them from its own records.

Constraints preserved in the message: all must_state items are covered (18 Sep return and £710.40 balance, letter linkage, no fee; 21 Sep re-presentation paid ref NL-9LIME-0442 after the 20 Sep salary; bank record proves debit only; do not pay yet and verify via trusted contact, not the text; no bank action, mandate unchanged, next collection not before 18 Oct; report back if Northgate deny the text). must_not items are respected: the message does not tell her to pay manually, does not claim the agency has reconciled the payment, does not call the text genuine or a scam, does not promise a refund or Guarantee claim, does not suggest cancelling or amending the mandate, and does not quote the text's contact details as trusted.

REASONING.md

1. Situation and history

Tomás Ribeiro, personal current account holder at Harrow & Vale Bank plc, contacted the bank on 2026-09-16 through the authenticated in-app personal banking chat (event-002). He reports that a holding deposit for a flat "went through twice", £634 each, that the letting agency promised a refund in 5–10 working days two days earlier, and that he needs the money for the actual deposit. He asks the bank to "just do a chargeback on the second one now" and says he has read that he is entitled to it. After the servicing agent confirmed seeing both payments and offered to explain how a chargeback would work and whether it is the fastest route (event-003), the customer replied that he does not want to wait 10 days for the agency (event-004).

Authenticated bank records (event-001, internal account view; attachments/card_transactions.csv) show two debit card payments to PAYGATE*NORTHGATE LET (MCC 6513, real estate agents): 2026-09-11 18:02:37, £634.00, auth A71C02; and 2026-09-11 18:04:12, £634.00, auth A71C09. Both have status "settled 2026-09-13". No PAYGATE refund credit appears in the snapshot.

The customer's own account (event-001, event-002) is that he clicked the agency's payment link twice after a "page not responding" message — i.e. both payments were initiated by him. This is the customer's report, consistent with the two distinct authorisation codes in the bank record.

The customer forwarded an agency email (attachments/agency_refund_email.eml) from Northgate Lettings (accounts@northgate-lettings.example, signed "Nicole"), dated Mon 14 Sep 2026 15:22:10 +0100, subject "RE: Holding deposit paid twice - 9 Lime Grove". It acknowledges the duplicate £634.00 holding deposit taken on 11 September and states the agency's payments team "will refund one payment to the card it was paid from within 5-10 working days", visible as a refund from PAYGATE.

The full input is saved in context.md.

2. Evidence and gaps

Established from the record

  • Two separately authorised, settled payments of £634 each on 11 Sep (card_transactions.csv, event-001).
  • Both initiated by the customer (event-001, event-002). Neither is unauthorised or fraudulent on the supplied evidence.
  • A written merchant refund commitment dated 14 Sep with a 5–10 working day window (agency_refund_email.eml). As at 16 Sep, that window has not elapsed, and no refund shows on the card record (card_transactions.csv).
  • Identity: the customer is interacting through the authenticated in-app chat and the internal account view identifies him as the account holder (event-001, event-002). Identity authority is established.
  • No dispute has been raised in the supplied record (event-003 only offers an explanation).

Not established / missing

  • Harrow & Vale Bank's card dispute policy and the applicable card-scheme rule on raising a duplicate / "credit not processed" dispute while the merchant has already committed in writing to a refund and the refund window is still running. This is not supplied, so eligibility cannot be decided at the servicing stage.
  • Whether any PAYGATE refund has posted after the transaction snapshot.
  • Any bank-committed dispute-processing timescale (none supplied), so no timing can be promised.
  • The customer's belief that he is "entitled" to a chargeback (event-002) is his understanding, not a rule in the supplied material; the record neither confirms nor denies it.

Contradictions: none in the facts. The tension is between the customer's expectation (immediate chargeback) and the record (self-initiated duplicate, merchant refund already promised and still within its window).

3. Why this plan

s1 — Request Card Disputes team action. The customer has made a clear dispute request. The record identifies the exact transaction (date/time, amount, merchant, MCC, auth code A71C09, settlement date) and the supporting evidence (customer's duplicate-click statement; the agency's 14 Sep email; absence of a refund at 16 Sep). What is missing is the eligibility rule: whether a duplicate/credit-not-processed dispute may be raised now or must wait for the merchant's refund window to expire. The plan's criterion (source: skill:payments handling rule, not a supplied bank policy or legal obligation) is to identify the transaction precisely, treat a refund promise as not a completed refund, and request the named team's action for a material gap rather than guess eligibility or outcome. Referring the request with full evidence lets the responsible team apply the scheme rules and bank policy that the planner does not have. The instruction expressly bars presenting this to the customer as a raised chargeback until the team acknowledges.

s2 — Reply to the customer. The customer believes he is entitled to an immediate chargeback and does not want to wait (event-002, event-004). The reply must (a) state what the record proves — two separately authorised payments, both settled 13 Sep, the agency's written commitment and the fact its window has not passed and no refund is visible; (b) correct the expectation that a chargeback is an instant, guaranteed or necessarily faster refund, without asserting either an entitlement or a denial; (c) report the dispute referral as pending, not raised; and (d) give practical steps (keep the email, watch for a PAYGATE refund, contact the agency to expedite). The criterion (skill:payments handling rule) is to state only the stage the record proves, promise no arrival times absent an applicable commitment, and report a planned operation as pending until its execution is acknowledged.

Order. s2 requires s1 because the customer reply describes the referral; the wording must match the actual status of s1. Until s1 is acknowledged, the message must say the request is being passed / pending, not that it has been passed. The plan's own draft uses "I have passed your request"; that wording is held back as a NOT SENT draft until s1 is acknowledged (see future_messages).

Alternatives. Raising a chargeback immediately at the servicing level is not supported: eligibility rules are not supplied and the merchant has already committed in writing to refund within a window that is still running. Simply declining and telling the customer to wait is also not chosen, because the customer has made a legitimate dispute request and the bank has a dedicated team to assess it. The plan takes the middle path: refer with full evidence, manage expectations honestly.

4. Execution boundaries

  • s1 is a request to the Card Disputes team to log and assess; it is not a raised chargeback. Whether a dispute is raised, and when, is the team's decision.
  • s2 depends on s1. The customer message may only describe the referral as completed after s1 is acknowledged; otherwise it stays in pending language.
  • The plan stops after s2. The next step depends on new information: the Card Disputes team's assessment result (to be reported to the servicing agent for onward communication) or a PAYGATE refund posting to the card. Neither outcome is predicted here. If s1 cannot be executed, a fresh decision is needed rather than an alternative route invented at this stage.
  • No safety flags; no escalate-if conditions in the plan.

5. Expected result and limits

What the steps could achieve: the dispute request reaches the team able to assess it under the applicable rules, with the complete evidence pack; the customer receives an accurate picture of both payments, the agency's commitment, and what a chargeback is and is not, and knows what to watch for and whom else to contact.

What remains unconfirmed: whether a dispute can be raised now or only after the refund window expires; whether the agency's refund will arrive, and when; any dispute outcome or credit date; whether a chargeback would be faster than the agency refund.

Constraints preserved: the message states the five must-state points (separately authorised payments with different auth codes, settled 13 Sep; agency's written 5–10 working day commitment not yet elapsed and no refund visible; chargeback is a team-assessed scheme dispute with no promised timing or guarantee; dispute request pending with nothing raised or credited; keep the email, watch for PAYGATE refund, may contact the agency). It does not assert legal entitlement or a bank obligation to refund, does not promise an outcome, date or speed, does not describe either payment as unauthorised or fraud, does not claim the dispute has been raised or a refund received, and does not request PINs, passwords, card numbers or one-time codes.

I paid a holding deposit for a flat and it went through twice — £634 each. The agency said they'd refund it in 5–10 working days but that was two days ago and I need the money for the actual deposit. Can you just do a chargeback on the second one now? I've read that I'm entitled to it.

ANSWER.md

Decision

Escalate. Raise a Direct Debit Guarantee claim for the 14 Sep 2026 collection of £4,980.00 (H&V COLLECT / LIME PROPERTY, ref LP-14GROVE-0208) with the Direct Debit disputes/indemnity team, request cancellation of the mandate on the customer's authenticated instruction, ask the customer for tenancy/move-in paperwork, and give the customer an accurate pending status. No refund is promised for today and nothing has been executed yet.

Expected disposition: escalated.

Proposed actions (in order)

  1. s1 – Request team action (Direct Debit disputes / indemnity team): Ask the team to assess a Direct Debit Guarantee claim for the 14 Sep 2026 collection of £4,980.00 on Tunde Okoye's personal current account (SYN-ACCT-15-1). Pass: the customer disputes the amount (agreed rent £795; all move-in money already paid); the 28 Aug 2026 Faster Payment HV26082811320471 of £4,980.00 to HARROW & VALE RE LIME PROPERTY CA ref MOVE IN 14 GROVE (completed; agency allocation not shown in bank record); mandate created 2 Sep via originator invite; 9 Sep advance notice stating £4,980.00 on 14 Sep then £795 monthly, which the collection matched; customer says he did not read the notice. Ask the team to determine whether this is handled as an indemnity claim or referred to the originator, and to review the unarranged overdraft daily fees (£6.50/day from 14 Sep; balance −£3,084.20 against a £1,000 arranged limit). Customer paperwork from s3 to follow.
  2. s2 – Request team action (Payments servicing team): Cancel Direct Debit instruction LP-14GROVE-0208 on the customer's authenticated phone instruction of 14 Sep 2026. Record that the customer is to be told the next scheduled collection (£795 on 14 Oct) will not be taken once cancelled, and that cancellation does not reverse the 14 Sep collection.
  3. s3 – Ask customer: Request a copy of the tenancy agreement or Lime Property move-in statement/invoice showing the agreed move-in sum and the £795 monthly rent, to attach to the Guarantee claim.
  4. s4 – Reply to customer (after s1–s3 are acknowledged): status message below.

Message to the customer (proposed, pending execution of s1–s3)

> I'm raising a Direct Debit Guarantee claim for the £4,980 collected today by Lime Property via H&V Collect (reference LP-14GROVE-0208) with our Direct Debit disputes team for assessment. I have to be straight with you: the amount and date taken match the advance notice sent on 9 September, so the team will need to assess whether this counts as an error under the Guarantee — I can't promise the money back today, and nothing has been refunded yet. I'm recording that you never agreed to £4,980 by Direct Debit and that you paid £4,980 by transfer on 28 August (ref MOVE IN 14 GROVE). Cancelling doesn't reverse today's collection or change what the agency says you owe. > > Separately, please contact Lime Property now about the apparent double payment and ask them to refund it or explain how they allocated your 28 August transfer. > > Your account is in unarranged overdraft and a £6.50 daily fee is being charged; I'm asking for those fees to be reviewed with the claim, but no waiver is confirmed. The plan's own wording of this message (which describes the claim and cancellation request as already raised) is held as a NOT SENT draft for use only once s1, s2 and s3 have been acknowledged as executed.

What is not being done

  • No same-day refund and no statement that the Guarantee automatically entitles him to one.
  • No finding that the collection was, or was not, an error by the originator or the bank.
  • No statement that the mandate is already cancelled or that fees are waived.
  • No suggestion that the 28 Aug Faster Payment can be recalled or has been refunded.

REASONING.md

1. Situation and history

All events are dated 2026-09-14 (full input in context.md).

  • event-001 (internal account view, authenticated bank record): Tunde Okoye, personal current account. Chronology: 28 Aug Faster Payment −£4,980.00 to HARROW & VALE RE LIME PROPERTY CA, ref "MOVE IN 14 GROVE" (bank record does not show how the agency allocated it); 2 Sep DD mandate H&V COLLECT / LIME PROPERTY created via originator invite; 9 Sep advance notice (originator copy) for £4,980.00 on 14 Sep then £795 monthly; 14 Sep DD collection −£4,980.00 paid. The note records the customer's stated rent of £795 and that he says he did not read the advance notice.
  • event-002 (phone, ID&V passed): customer reports the agency took "nearly five grand" by Direct Debit, says he paid all move-in money by transfer two weeks ago, rent is £795, he is heavily overdrawn, and wants a full refund today under the Guarantee and the DD cancelled.
  • event-003: agent acknowledges and says they will check the mandate and notice.
  • event-004: customer states he never agreed to £4,980 by Direct Debit, regardless of the notice.

Attachments:

  • attachments/dd_advance_notice_2026-09-09.eml — originator notice dated 9 Sep 2026 to t.okoye@example.net: reference LP-14GROVE-0208; first collection £4,980.00 on 14 Sep 2026 "(balance of your account as at 8 September)", then £795.00 monthly on the 14th; instructs contacting Lime Property before the collection date if any amount is incorrect.
  • attachments/payment_confirmation_2026-08-28.pdf — bank confirmation of Faster Payment HV26082811320471, 28 Aug 2026 11:32, £4,980.00, payee HARROW & VALE RE LIME PROPERTY CA, ref MOVE IN 14 GROVE, status Completed, account SYN-ACCT-15-1. It states it is not a receipt from the beneficiary.
  • attachments/statement_extract.csv — 28 Aug FP −£4,980.00 (balance £1,902.30); 2 Sep DD instruction set up LP-14GROVE-0208; 14 Sep DD −£4,980.00 (balance −£3,077.70); 14 Sep unarranged overdraft daily fee −£6.50 (balance −£3,084.20).

Customer report vs authenticated records: the bank records confirm the 28 Aug payment, the 2 Sep mandate, the 9 Sep notice content, and the 14 Sep collection. The customer's claims that the agreed rent is £795, that all move-in money was already covered by the 28 Aug transfer, and that he never agreed to £4,980 by DD are his assertions only; nothing supplied confirms or contradicts the agreed amounts.

2. Evidence and gaps

  • Identity: ID&V passed on the phone channel (event-002); the account holder is confirmed in event-001. Identity authority is established, so the cancellation instruction can be acted on.
  • The collection matches the advance notice exactly in amount, date and reference (dd_advance_notice_2026-09-09.eml vs statement_extract.csv). This matters for how the Guarantee claim is assessed, because the notice described the £4,980 as the "balance of your account as at 8 September" — i.e. the originator presented it as money owed. Whether that balance was itself wrong (because the 28 Aug transfer should have cleared it) cannot be determined from the bank's records; the payment confirmation explicitly says it is not a receipt from the beneficiary, and event-001 says the allocation is not shown.
  • Minor inconsistency: event-001 says the account is "£3,100 overdrawn", while the statement extract shows −£3,084.20 after the fee. The plan uses the statement figures; the difference is small and does not change the decision.
  • Gaps listed in the plan: tenancy/move-in paperwork showing agreed amounts; how Lime Property allocated the 28 Aug payment; how the 2 Sep mandate was completed and what amount (if any) the customer agreed at setup; the bank's internal DD indemnity procedure and timescales; the bank's policy on refunding unarranged overdraft fees arising from a disputed collection. None of the last three are supplied, so no timescale, refund entitlement or fee waiver can be stated.
  • No safety flags are raised; there is no indication of fraud or third-party takeover — the originator is the customer's own letting agency and the mandate was set up via an originator invite on 2 Sep.

3. Why this plan

s1 – Guarantee claim to the DD disputes/indemnity team. The customer has expressly invoked the Guarantee (event-002). The plan's rationale: the collection matched the advance notice, but the customer asserts the amount was never agreed and the same sum was already paid by transfer, so whether an "error" occurred needs assessment under the bank's process rather than an immediate refund promise. Evidence: event-002, event-004, the advance notice, the payment confirmation and the statement extract. Criterion: identify the alleged error and the applicable route without promising a refund for every supplier dispute, and keep the claim separate from changing future instructions. Source: the scheduled-payments handling skill (a handling rule, not a supplied bank policy or statutory clause). The step also asks the team to review the £6.50/day unarranged overdraft fee as a consequence of the disputed collection; because no fee policy is supplied, this is a review request only. s1 is listed first because the refund question is the customer's primary demand and the most time-sensitive given the overdraft.

s2 – Cancel mandate LP-14GROVE-0208. The ID&V-passed account holder gave an explicit cancellation instruction (event-002). Rationale: this is a supported change on the customer's authority and is independent of the Guarantee claim. Criterion: cancel on the customer's authority; cancellation does not establish that a collected payment was reversed or that a contract ended. Source: scheduled-payments handling skill. The step records that the customer must be told the £795 due on 14 Oct will not be taken once cancelled and that cancellation does not reverse the 14 Sep collection — so he is not left thinking rent is being paid, or that the collection is undone.

s3 – Ask the customer for tenancy/move-in paperwork. Rationale: the bank's records show the collection matched the notice; the customer's assertion that the amount was not owed needs documentary support of the agreed amounts. Criterion: where a gap affects the decision, name the precise missing fact and make a concrete request (general evidence rule). This feeds the s1 claim ("customer paperwork from s3 to follow").

s4 – Reply to the customer. Rationale: the customer needs an accurate status — claim and cancellation requested but pending, no refund promised, what cancellation does and does not do, the fee position, and the direct route with the agency. Criterion: reply with recorded status and what it does not confirm; a request to change is not a confirmed change and a review request does not establish a refund. Source: complaints-rights handling skill, which also grounds the complaint/Ombudsman signposting (without a fixed deadline or predicted outcome). The mention of the Financial Ombudsman Service is the plan's signposting of an external route, not a statement of a legal obligation or outcome. s4 depends on s1–s3 because the message reports those actions as raised.

Alternatives: an immediate same-day refund under the Guarantee is what the customer asked for, but the supplied records show the collection matched the advance notice and the error question is unresolved, and the bank's indemnity procedure is not supplied — so the plan does not promise it. Refusing the claim outright is equally unsupported, because the apparent double payment of the same £4,980 is a genuine disputed point. Routing through the disputes team is the only path supported by the evidence.

4. Execution boundaries

  • s1, s2 and s3 have no prerequisites and can be raised in parallel; s4 requires acknowledgement of all three.
  • The plan's s4 wording describes the claim and cancellation as already raised ("I've raised…", "I've requested…"). That wording is appropriate only after s1–s3 are acknowledged as executed, so it is held as a NOT SENT draft in future_messages. The message in ANSWER.md uses present-state wording ("I'm raising…", "I'm requesting…") and covers the same must-state points.
  • The plan stops at: the disputes team's determination (indemnity claim vs referral to originator), the fee review outcome, confirmation of mandate cancellation, and receipt of the customer's paperwork. Any of these requires a fresh decision; no further branches are added here.
  • No escalation conditions are set (escalate_if empty); the disposition is already escalated to the two internal teams.

5. Expected result and limits

What the steps can achieve: the Guarantee claim is formally in the bank's assessment process with the relevant evidence; the mandate cancellation is requested so no further collections (including the £795 on 14 Oct) are taken once confirmed; the customer knows exactly what is pending, what to send, that he should pursue Lime Property directly about the apparent double payment, and that the fees are under review.

What remains unconfirmed: whether a refund will be made and when; whether the collection was an error by the originator or the bank; whether the unarranged overdraft fees will be refunded; whether and when the cancellation takes effect; how Lime Property allocated the 28 Aug transfer.

Constraints preserved: the message states the claim is raised for assessment and not decided, that the collection matched the 9 Sep notice, that his position is recorded, that cancellation is requested but unconfirmed and does not reverse the collection or affect what he owes the agency, that he should contact Lime Property directly, the fee position and review, and the complaint/Ombudsman route. It does not promise a same-day refund, does not characterise the collection as an error or not, does not say the DD is cancelled or fees waived, does not suggest the 28 Aug Faster Payment can be recalled, does not imply cancellation ends his tenancy obligations, and gives no complaint deadline or Ombudsman prediction.

Plan

Intent: Authenticated customer disputes a £4,980 Direct Debit collected on 14 Sep by Lime Property via H&V Collect (he says the move-in money was already paid by Faster Payment on 28 Aug and the agreed rent is £795), wants a full refund today under the Direct Debit Guarantee and wants the mandate cancelled.

ANSWER.md

Decision

Release only what the recorded authority covers. Authority TPA-2024-00931 (Form TPA-2, signed by Christine H. 12 March 2024, processed 14 March 2024) permits Dawes & Co, contact Michael Dawes, to receive statements and balance information for the sole account SYN-ACCT-16-1 only. The requester's email address matches the contact recorded on that authority.

  • Sole account SYN-ACCT-16-1, 6 April 2025 – 5 April 2026: propose generation and delivery to Dawes & Co via the bank's supported third-party delivery route (pending; nothing has been generated or sent).
  • Joint account SYN-ACCT-16-2: decline. The authority expressly excludes any joint account; a TPA-2 on a joint account requires all holders to sign and David H. has never signed one.
  • Per-property rental statements: decline. The bank does not hold or produce these.
  • Inform Christine H. through her registered secure channel so she and David can decide whether to sign a new TPA-2 for the joint account.

Expected disposition: completed (no escalation conditions).

Proposed actions (in order; none executed)

  1. s1 – Confirm identity and authority (case record). Record that the requester (Michael Dawes, m.dawes@dawesandco.example) matches the named contact and email on TPA-2024-00931; that the authority covers statements and balance information for SYN-ACCT-16-1 only; that no authority exists for SYN-ACCT-16-2; and that the authority does not extend to any other information about Christine H.
  2. s2 – Hold/decline (case record). Decline the joint account SYN-ACCT-16-2 statements (no third-party authority; all holders must sign; David H. has never signed) and the per-property rental statements (not bank records). Do not release any joint account information or the identity of the managing agent.
  3. s3 – Request team action (statements / third-party requests team) — requires s1. Ask the team to generate statements for sole account SYN-SC-16-1 / SYN-ACCT-16-1 covering 6 April 2025 to 5 April 2026 and deliver them to Dawes & Co (Michael Dawes, m.dawes@dawesandco.example) under TPA-2024-00931, using the bank's supported delivery route for TPA-2 third-party statement releases. Scope strictly to SYN-ACCT-16-1.
  4. s4 – Reply to the accountant (email) — requires s2 and s3. Send the message below.
  5. s5 – Notify the account holder (Christine H., registered secure channel) — requires s2. Tell her that Dawes & Co requested statements for her joint account and per-property rental statements; that only the sole account statements are being provided under TPA-2024-00931; that a joint account authority requires a new TPA-2 signed by both her and David H. via the bank's branch/authority process; and that per-property rental statements are not held by the bank.

Proposed message to Michael Dawes, Dawes & Co (not yet sent)

> Thank you for your request. Under the authority Christine holds with us (ref TPA-2024-00931), we are arranging statements for her sole account ending 16-1 for 6 April 2025 to 5 April 2026; these will follow via our standard third-party delivery route and have not yet been sent. We are unable to provide any statements for the joint account: the authority on file covers only the sole account, and a third-party authority on a joint account must be signed by all account holders. If Christine and David wish Dawes & Co to receive joint account statements, they would both need to complete a new TPA-2 form for that account through the bank. We also do not hold or produce property-by-property rental statements; Christine would need to obtain those from whoever manages her lettings.

Not included / not permitted

  • No statement, balance or transaction information for joint account SYN-ACCT-16-2.
  • No disclosure of the property management agency's name or any other details beyond SYN-ACCT-16-1 statements.
  • No claim that statements have been sent or delivered.
  • The accountant's "as usual" remark is not treated as a verified record of prior releases.
  • No joint authority is set up on the account holders' behalf at the accountant's request.

REASONING.md

REASONING — Case 016: Accountant requests statements (Harrow & Vale Bank plc)

Full input is saved in context.md. Nothing in this memo has been executed; every action below is proposed.

1. Situation and history

Authenticated bank records

  • event-001 (2026-09-24, internal account view): Christine H. holds two accounts: sole "Rental income" account SYN-SC-16-1 / SYN-ACCT-16-1 and a joint account with spouse David H., SYN-SC-16-1 / SYN-ACCT-16-2. A third-party authority signed by Christine in March 2024 allows Dawes & Co to request statements for SYN-ACCT-16-1. No authority exists on the joint account; David H. has never signed one. The bank holds no per-property rental statements — those are produced by the system of Oakfield Property Management, which manages Christine's lets.
  • attachments/third_party_authority_2024.pdf: Form TPA-2 (account information only), ref TPA-2024-00931, account holder Christine H., signed 12/03/2024, witnessed and processed at H&V York branch 14/03/2024. It authorises the bank to provide "statements and balance information for the account named above" to Dawes & Co Chartered Accountants, contact Michael Dawes, m.dawes@dawesandco.example. It states the authority "does not permit the third party to make payments, change details, or obtain information about any other account I hold, including any joint account", and the form notes "one authority per account. A joint account requires all account holders to sign." The named account is SYN-ACCT-16-1 (sole account).

Third-party report (unverified beyond the sender match)

  • event-002 / attachments/accountant_request.eml (Thu 24 Sep 2026 09:41 +0100): Michael Dawes, from m.dawes@dawesandco.example, asks "as usual" for Christine's statements for the tax year to 5 April 2026, and this year for both the rental account and the joint account with David, plus property-by-property rental statements for six lets.

2. Evidence and gaps

  • Requester identity vs. authority: the sender address and name in accountant_request.eml match the contact recorded on TPA-2024-00931. The plan treats identity/authority as established on this basis. Residual uncertainty: a matching email address is not strong authentication on its own; this is mitigated because the only release proposed is one the authority already permits and it is routed through the bank's supported third-party delivery route rather than as a reply attachment.
  • Scope of the authority: the PDF is explicit that it covers SYN-ACCT-16-1 only and excludes any joint account. event-001 independently confirms no authority exists for SYN-ACCT-16-2 and that David H. has never signed one. There is no contradiction between the two records.
  • "As usual": the accountant implies prior releases. No record of any previous release is supplied (listed in the plan's missing). Prior practice, even if true, would not widen the authority's scope, so this gap does not affect the decision.
  • Per-property statements: event-001 records that the bank holds none; they originate from the managing agent's system. The request therefore asks for records the bank cannot produce.
  • Delivery route: the bank's documented route for TPA-2 statement releases is not supplied. The plan accordingly instructs the statements team to use the supported route rather than assuming email attachment.
  • Period: the request is for "the last tax year" / "tax year to 5 Apr 2026"; the plan maps this to 6 April 2025 – 5 April 2026 (UK tax year). This is an interpretation of the request, not a figure from a record, but it is the only reasonable reading of the subject line.

3. Why this plan

s1 – confirm_identity_authority (source: skill:privacy-documents; evidence: third_party_authority_2024.pdf, event-001, accountant_request.eml). Criterion: limit disclosure to the request and established authority, matching document type (statements), period, account and recipient. The authority document and the sender match jointly establish entitlement for SYN-ACCT-16-1 and nothing else. This is a handling rule of the privacy-documents skill; the account-by-account scope itself is set by the bank's own TPA-2 form text.

s2 – hold_or_decline (source: general:safety; evidence: event-001, third_party_authority_2024.pdf). Criterion: where there is a privacy/authority gap, hold the disclosure and limit information to the established authority. The joint account is excluded by the bank's form in terms, and the form's own rule ("A joint account requires all account holders to sign") is unmet because David H. has never signed. The per-property statements are not bank records, so there is nothing to release. The decline also covers the managing agent's identity: the authority covers account information for SYN-ACCT-16-1, not other facts about Christine's affairs. This is a general safety handling rule applied to a supplied bank document; no external statute is cited because none was supplied.

s3 – request_team_action (source: skill:privacy-documents; evidence: PDF, event-001, event-002; requires s1). Criterion: use an authorised, supported delivery route and match period and account to the authority. The release is scoped to SYN-ACCT-16-1 for 6 April 2025 – 5 April 2026 and to the named contact on the authority. It depends on s1 so that the entitlement is recorded before any generation is requested.

s4 – reply_to_customer (source: general:planner; evidence: eml, PDF, event-001; requires s2 and s3). Criterion: reply with the recorded status and what it does not confirm, disclosing only within established authority. The message gives the accountant a clear split (arranged / declined / not held), states the sole-account statements have not yet been sent, explains the all-holders rule, and points to a new TPA-2 via the bank without naming the managing agent or any joint account detail. It is ordered after s2 and s3 so it reflects both the decline and the pending team request.

s5 – route_to_secure_process (source: general:safety; evidence: event-001, PDF; requires s2). Criterion: identify a supported secure route for extending authority instead of accepting a third party's request to widen access. Only the account holders can extend authority; informing Christine via her registered secure channel lets her and David decide whether to sign a joint TPA-2 and keeps the accountant from acting beyond the recorded authority.

Alternatives considered within the evidence: releasing the joint account statements on the strength of the accountant's statement that Christine "asked us to do the full return" is not supported — the form requires all holders' signatures, and a third party's assertion cannot substitute. Declining everything pending contact with Christine would be unnecessarily restrictive, since the sole-account release is squarely within a valid, uncancelled authority. The plan's split is the proportionate position.

4. Execution boundaries

  • Dependency order: s1 → s3; s2 → s4 and s5; s3 → s4. s1 and s2 are case-record entries with no prerequisites.
  • The plan stops after s5. It does not set up any authority for the joint account, does not contact David H., and does not pursue the per-property statements. Any future joint-account release would require a new TPA-2 signed by both holders through the bank's branch/authority process — a new decision on new records, not part of this plan.
  • If the statements team cannot identify a supported TPA-2 delivery route, s3 is blocked and a fresh decision is needed; this memo does not propose a substitute route.
  • escalate_if is empty; no escalation conditions apply.

5. Expected result and limits

What the steps could achieve: a recorded authority check; a documented decline of the out-of-scope items; a pending request to the statements team for SYN-ACCT-16-1 statements for 6 April 2025 – 5 April 2026 to Dawes & Co under TPA-2024-00931; a reply to the accountant setting out the split; and a secure notification to Christine enabling her and David to extend authority if they choose.

What remains unconfirmed: generation and delivery of the sole-account statements (nothing has been sent); whether any prior releases to Dawes & Co occurred; whether Christine and David will sign a joint TPA-2; and the specific delivery route the team will use.

Constraints preserved (must_state / must_not): the reply states that sole-account statements are being arranged and not yet sent, that joint account statements cannot be provided because the authority covers only SYN-ACCT-16-1 and a joint authority needs all holders, that the bank does not hold per-property rental statements, and that both holders must complete a new TPA-2 through the bank. It discloses no joint account information, does not name the property management agency, does not claim delivery, does not rely on "as usual" as a record, does not imply the bank can produce per-property statements, and does not accept a joint authority set-up at the accountant's request.

The true end-of-day close for 29 Sep was £55,647.48, after a further £120.00 A ROSS credit (ref INV 4480 PART) at 16:41:09.

REASONING — Case 017: Statement balance query (Bright Sparks Electrical Ltd)

1. Situation and history

  • event-001 (2026-09-30, internal account view, Harrow & Vale Bank plc): Authenticated case record for business current account SYN-SC-17-1 / SYN-ACCT-17-1. It records that the CSV export was requested by user violetta.k at 13:15:02 on 29 Sep; that two credits booked after that time (+£1,750.00 at 14:02, K WHITTLE, ref "INV 4471"; +£120.00 at 16:41, A ROSS, ref "INV 4480 PART"); that the customer's quoted closing balance matches the running balance between 14:02 and 16:41; and that the same pattern occurred on 28 Sep (+£1,900.00 at 18:10, after a 13:20 export).
  • event-002 (2026-09-30, business banking chat, Violetta — bookkeeper): Customer report: £1,750 out on 29 Sep balancing; download shows £30,078.94 credits but the closing only works with £31,828.94; asks for a missing-payment investigation and suggests "something's been lost".
  • event-003 (2026-09-30, chat, servicing agent): Agent states they will compare the export with full-day activity. No results are recorded beyond the attachments.
  • event-004 (2026-09-30, chat, Violetta): The director wants a formal missing-funds complaint logged if it isn't found today.
  • Attachments: attachments/bookkeeper_reconciliation_2026-09-29.csv (customer's working), attachments/export_2026-09-29_1315.csv (customer's download), attachments/full_day_2026-09-29.csv (bank's end-of-day view).

Contact started 09:40 local UK time on 30 Sep; individual message times are not recorded (request.md).

Customer report vs authenticated record: The claim that a credit is "missing" or "lost" (event-002) is a customer assertion. The authenticated records (event-001 and full_day_2026-09-29.csv) show every credit booked.

2. Evidence and gaps

Reconciliation of the figures (all from supplied files):

  • export_2026-09-29_1315.csv is stamped exported_at 2026-09-29T13:15:02 and lists four credits (£875.00 J MORGAN INV 4462; £1,200.00 CARDNET SETTLEMENT 2809; £27,343.94 HALDANE HOMES LTD INV 4455 STAGE 2; £660.00 S AHMED INV 4470) totalling £30,078.94, and the supplier batch debit of −£8,415.30 at 11:05:12, leaving a running balance of £53,777.48. This matches line 4 of the bookkeeper's reconciliation exactly.
  • bookkeeper_reconciliation_2026-09-29.csv uses a closing balance of £55,527.48 (line 5) with the note "Export taken 13:15; balance noted from Business Online dashboard later that afternoon". Line 6 difference: £1,750.00.
  • full_day_2026-09-29.csv shows the same five items as the export, then £1,750.00 credit, K WHITTLE, INV 4471, 14:02:47, running balance £55,527.48, then £120.00 credit, A ROSS, INV 4480 PART, 16:41:09, running balance £55,647.48.
  • The £1,750.00 difference therefore equals, to the penny, the K WHITTLE credit booked 47 minutes after the export; the dashboard balance the bookkeeper used is the running balance between 14:02:47 and 16:41:09. The customer's implied credit figure of £31,828.94 (event-002) = £30,078.94 + £1,750.00, consistent with this.
  • The customer's 29 Sep opening balance of £32,113.84 matches the running balance after the £1,900.00 P LEWIS credit (INV 4461) at 18:10:22 on 28 Sep in full_day_2026-09-29.csv — the same post-export timing pattern noted in event-001.

Contradictions: None between the authenticated records. The only discrepancy is between the customer's inference ("something's been lost") and the records, and it is fully explained by the cut-off timing.

Gaps (per the plan's missing):

  • Whether the director still wants a formal complaint after the explanation — the request in event-004 was conditional on the funds not being found, and that condition is not met on the records. This cannot be assumed either way.
  • Exact 30 Sep chat message times are not recorded (only the 09:40 contact start).
  • Event-003 records an intention to compare; no execution or output beyond the supplied attachments is recorded. The plan's conclusion rests on the attachments and event-001, not on any recorded agent investigation.

Identity/authority: The plan records identity authority as established — Violetta is the bookkeeper, the export was requested under violetta.k on the same account (event-001), and the request is for information she already has access to with no account change requested. No safety flags were raised.

3. Why this plan

Step s1 — reply_to_customer (reconciliation explanation).

  • *Explanation (from the plan):* The export, reconciliation and full-day record identify the exact transaction (£1,750.00 K WHITTLE INV 4471 at 14:02:47) and show the difference is a cut-off timing mismatch, not a missing credit; the record also shows a second post-export credit (£120.00 A ROSS) that the customer has not yet accounted for, which is why the true end-of-day close (£55,647.48) is also stated.
  • *Evidence cited:* attachments/export_2026-09-29_1315.csv, attachments/bookkeeper_reconciliation_2026-09-29.csv, attachments/full_day_2026-09-29.csv, event-001, event-002.
  • *Criterion:* Identify the exact transaction by reference, amount, date and parties and use the supplied authoritative record directly; state only what the record proves.
  • *Source:* skill:payments — this is a skill handling rule, not a supplied bank policy document or a legal obligation.
  • The 28 Sep pattern and the export-after-end-of-day suggestion are included because event-001 records the same pattern and the practical advice follows directly from the evidence; it is advice on the customer's own process, not a promise of any system change.

Step s2 — ask_customer (complaint confirmation).

  • *Explanation (from the plan):* The complaint request in event-004 was conditional on the funds not being found; the records show them found, so the customer's current wish must be confirmed rather than assumed, while preserving the unconditional right to complain.
  • *Evidence cited:* event-004, request.
  • *Criterion:* Recognise and route a complaint separately from the operational query; do not make registration conditional on abandoning the dispute; include only acknowledgements actually available.
  • *Source:* skill:complaints-rights — a skill handling rule. No bank complaints policy or regulatory text was supplied in this case, so no clause is cited.
  • No complaint flag is raised at this snapshot because the stated condition ("if it isn't found today") is not met on the records; the plan still offers registration unconditionally and forbids refusing or delaying it on the basis that the explanation resolves the query.

Order: s1 precedes s2 so that the customer has the located transaction before being asked whether the conditional complaint request still stands. Neither step has a requires dependency or condition; both are messages in the same chat.

Alternatives considered only as supported by the plan: Logging the complaint immediately was not selected because the request was expressly conditional and the condition is not met; refusing/closing the complaint route was not selected because the right to complain is unconditional. The plan does not propose any payment trace, recall or investigation because the records show nothing is missing.

4. Execution boundaries

  • Both actions are proposed and not executed. No message has been sent, no complaint has been registered, and no account operation is involved.
  • s2 explicitly stops the plan pending the customer's answer: no complaint is to be logged until Violetta confirms the director's wish. If she confirms, registration is the next decision to be taken on that confirmation; the plan does not pre-decide its content or outcome.
  • No escalation conditions (escalate_if is empty) and no safety flags.
  • The plan does not propose any change to Business Online export behaviour, and none should be implied.

What the steps could achieve: A complete, evidence-based explanation of the £1,750.00 difference, identification of the second post-export £120.00 credit and the correct end-of-day close (£55,647.48), a practical way to avoid the recurring cut-off mismatch, and a clear, unconditional invitation to confirm whether a formal complaint is still wanted.
